7 Netflix Mystery Shows That Hook You in the First Scene

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Leading the streaming crusade, Netflix has stood at the top of excellent content. From legendary shows during its early days like Orange Is the New Black to modern masterpieces like The Diplomat, viewers seek brilliant, risky storytelling for their binge-watching pleasure. The thrill of a good series is being drawn in so much that you'll keep watching until the very end. When it comes to Netflix's mystery options, a handful hook you within the first scene.

We are here to honor the incredible mystery-centric shows that hook you instantly. From a terrifying, disorienting wake-up that establishes the entire story to the discovery of a body that sets the mystery into hyperdrive, these shows master the art of immediate intrigue. While Netflix has some perfect shows that are incredible all the way through, they don't have the same brilliant hook as these seven entries.

Sometimes high-concept narratives scare people away, but Bodies is a highly addictive puzzle that pulls you in instantly. Created by Paul Tomalin, Bodies is based on the DC Vertigo graphic novel of the same name. The eight-episode sci-fi mystery thriller follows four different detectives in four distinct eras who all discover the exact same unidentified body in a London alleyway. As their investigations unfold across time, they realize their cases are deeply interconnected. Together, they must unravel a massive, century-spanning conspiracy that threatens humanity's future. A truly brilliant and gripping thriller, Bodies succeeds because you're dropped right into the seismic action.

To kick off the story, streetlights in London begin to flicker and pop just before Detective Sergeant Shahara Hasan (Amaka Okafor) is deployed to police a far-right protest. While monitoring the tense crowd, Shahara spots a suspicious teenage boy hiding behind a bin and carrying a gun. Against orders, she chases him through the streets until he leads her into Longharvest Lane in Whitechapel. There, instead of a simple cornered suspect, she discovers a naked, dead white male body lying on the ground with a gunshot wound to the eye. The victim doesn't just lie there cold—he gasps out a terrified, reality-bending statement before bolting, instantly elevating the mystery from a standard murder to something deeply unsettling. By plunging the audience into high-stakes, chaotic action that defies conventional crime-drama logic, the premiere makes it clear this mystery is unlike any other thriller you've seen. Rather than lingering as a slow-burn procedural, the premiere swiftly layers in parallel historical and futuristic teasers, creating an immediate puzzle of time and causality. It's an adrenaline-fueled opening that packs a thrilling punch.

While many people point to Squid Game as the foreign language thriller that changed the game, the German sci-fi mystery thriller Dark paved the way first. Created by Baran bo Odar and Jantje Friese, Dark follows four interconnected families in the small town of Winden as a child's disappearance uncovers a multi-generational time-travel conspiracy. As the characters search for the truth across linked timelines in 1953, 1986, and 2019, they become trapped in a deterministic loop of fate and cause and effect. For three strong seasons, Dark served as a brilliant time-travel drama that explores the existential implications of time and its effect on human nature and life.

In the first scene of the series, a voice-over recites a quote by Albert Einstein about time being an illusion, accompanied by a montage of ticking clocks, a complex cork board of interconnected strings and photos, and a dark study room. The sequence then moves directly to November 19, 2019, where Michael Kahnwald (Sebastian Rudolph) stands in his attic, seals a mysterious letter with strict instructions—"Do not open before November 4th at 10:13 p.m."—looks at a family photograph, and commits suicide by hanging himself. The shocking scene subverts the standard true-crime missing-persons formula and replaces it with cold, existential dread. The scene follows a calculated, deeply unsettling thematic blueprint that lets you know you are watching a tragedy already in motion. The camera lingers on this devastating, quiet moment, leaving you with urgent questions: why he did it, what's in the letter, and the significance of that specific date. By the time the hypnotic, mirrored opening credits roll to the tune of Apparat's "Goodbye," the show had already laid out its entire central puzzle.

A truly unique entry into the true crime genre, the psychological crime thriller Mindhunter, created by Joe Penhall, is based on Mindhunter: Inside the FBI's Elite Serial Crime Unit by John E. Douglas and Mark Olshaker. The series follows FBI agents Holden Ford (Jonathan Groff) and Bill Tench (Holt McCallany) as they pioneer the Behavioral Science Unit. Alongside psychologist Dr. Wendy Carr (Anna Torv), they interview incarcerated serial killers to understand their psychology and apply these insights to active murder cases. The series also explores the heavy emotional toll and institutional skepticism the team faces as it builds the foundation of modern criminal profiling. Tackling the crime genre in a unique way, this two-season series introduces a new way to analyze crime stories.

The series opens in Braddock, Pennsylvania, in 1977 with a tense hostage standoff that serves as the catalyst for the entire series. A deeply disturbed man named Cody Miller (David H. Holmes) robs a liquor store, steals a shotgun, and barricades himself inside a building with a woman and several other hostages. As local police bark orders, young FBI Special Agent Holden Ford arrives as the hostage negotiator. Holden tries to de-escalate the situation by establishing an empathetic, human connection with the suspect. He taps into the suspect's delusion as Cody demands to see his wife and begins shouting that he is literally invisible. Holden speaks softly, telling Cody that he can see he is cold, which coaxes Cody to pull his pants back up and take his primary hostage back inside. Holden learns Cody's wife is arriving, but advises against letting them speak. Realizing his demands aren't being met and that his "invisibility" is failing, Cody suddenly stops listening to Holden and blows his own head off with a shotgun. The scene closes with Holden returning home covered in blood, trying and failing to scrub the stain out of his white dress shirt.

The scene establishes the series' central conflict as Holden doesn't see the result as a success, left obsessed with the realization that traditional, authoritative FBI methods fail against abnormal psychology. Directed by David Fincher, the sudden, graphic shock instantly breaks traditional television formulas and signals to the audience that no one is safe. Holden's failure will eventually become his success, as the traditional rules of law enforcement are entirely useless against a changing landscape of irrational, deeply broken minds.

A seemingly forgotten series, The Madness is an action conspiracy thriller unlike anything you've ever experienced. Created by Stephen Belber, The Madness follows Muncie Daniels (Colman Domingo), a prominent media pundit and political commentator. While on a solo writing retreat in the Pocono Mountains, Muncie stumbles upon the body of a murdered white supremacist and is framed for the assassination. Forced to go on the run as a fugitive, he must navigate a web of mass disinformation and expose a deep political conspiracy to clear his name and keep his family safe. A high-octane thrill ride, The Madness is all about Domingo and his dominating performance from the jump.

In the opening scene, Muncie wakes up disoriented and alone in the Poconos woods, stumbles onto a nearby roadway, and flags down a ride. He goes to a local family restaurant where patrons stare at him, and he tells police officers Wagner (Hamish Allan-Headley) and Lutz (Dylan Taylor) he's had a rough morning. Suddenly, flashes of memory surface before he transitions to getting makeup done and preparing to host his CNN-style television program. The scene immediately grips you by using a flash-forward technique that forces instant empathy and high-stakes mystery. Rather than build to the crisis, the series drops you directly into the terrifying aftermath of a violent event. Injured, bloodied, and completely disoriented, the audience is instantly placed in his exact shoes as the protagonist. The peace is shattered as Muncie is shown frantically sprinting through the woods while a gunshot rings out in the distance. The shaky, claustrophobic cinematography mimics a raw survival instinct, generating immediate adrenaline and a palpable sense of danger. Then, by moving from frantic chaos to Muncie's normal life, you are desperate to figure out how an articulate, high-profile media analyst could possibly end up running for his life in a remote swamp.

Four Netflix characters, four completely different ways of showing up for somebody. This is the one who fits how you actually do friendship, and what having them in your life would be like.

You do not do friendship at half volume, and neither does he. What you want is somebody who says yes to the plan before you have finished describing the plan.

You are the friend who makes things happen, and so is she. Together you would be mildly exhausting to everyone around you and completely delighted with yourselves.

You have no patience for pleasant lies, and she has no ability to tell them. What looks like coldness from the outside is the most reliable friendship on this list.

You look after whoever is having the worst day. So does he. It is the least strategic way to move through the world and the best possible quality in a friend.

One of the greatest long-running Netflix series is The Night Agent. The highly addictive series, created by Shawn Ryan and based on the novel of the same name by Matthew Quirk, follows Peter Sutherland (Gabriel Basso), a low-level FBI agent who works in the basement of the White House monitoring a phone line that never rings—until the night it does. When a panicked tech CEO calls, Peter is thrust into a sprawling, fast-paced conspiracy involving a Russian mole at the highest levels of the U.S. government. As he runs to protect the witness and clear his own name, he uncovers secrets that lead all the way to the Oval Office. Moving into its fourth and final season, The Night Agent became an instant hit thanks to its simple yet evocative start.

In the opening sequence, FBI agent Peter Sutherland boards a DC Metro train during his commute and notices a suspicious man leaving a backpack behind, which he discovers contains a timer-triggered bomb. He immediately forces the train to make an emergency stop in a dark tunnel and helps panic-stricken passengers escape into the underground structures. The bomb detonates moments later, sending a massive fireball through the tunnel and killing one civilian while saving dozens of others thanks to Peter's quick actions. While recovering from the blast, Peter spots the bomber standing among the bystanders on the surface and gives chase, though the suspect manages to elude him after Peter is hurt in a car accident. This high-octane start raises the stakes beyond his life-saving moment. It paints the protagonist as a competent, brave, and quick-thinking hero. The frantic life-or-death scale of the flashback contrasts sharply with the quiet, mundane reality of his day job: sitting in a dark White House basement waiting for a silent emergency phone to ring. This quick-fire tragedy gives Peter instant sympathetic context and raises the emotional weight of every political secret and threat that follows.​​​​​​​

Easily the biggest travesty of 2025 was the cancellation of The Residence, as it was simply perfect. But while many fans mourn the loss of a brilliant vehicle for Uzo Aduba, let's honor why it was so good. Created by Paul William Davies and inspired by The Residence: Inside the Private World of the White House by Kate Andersen Brower, the Shondaland series is a comedic murder mystery set in the White House during a high-stakes state dinner. When the White House Chief Usher, A. B. Wynter (Giancarlo Esposito), is found dead, an eccentric and brilliant detective named Cordelia Cupp (Aduba) is brought in to solve the crime, sifting through 157 suspects among the mansion's staff and guests. The show follows Cupp as she teams up with skeptical FBI agent Edwin Park (Randall Park) to untangle the "upstairs, downstairs" whodunit. The Residence is a fun, cozy, campy romp that delivers the right amount of mystery from the start.

In the opening scene, A.B. Wynter is found dead by the president's mother-in-law, Nan Cox (Jane Curtin), at 9:57 p.m. after a strange thump is heard. The death is immediately presented as an apparent suicide, with slit wrists and a suicide note. The president's chief adviser, Harry Hollinger (Ken Marino), and other officials try to rush a suicide ruling to avoid a political scandal during the state visit. But once the eccentric Cordelia Cupp arrives, she quickly spots fatal inconsistencies, including signs of poisoning and evidence that the body was moved. The first scene hooks you because of its abrupt, jarring contrast between extreme high-society elegance and sudden, chaotic horror. However, the show immediately subverts this stately elegance by ripping the audience up to the third floor. The story instantly becomes the ultimate locked-room mystery where everyone, even world leaders, is potential suspects. It thrusts the story into the messy inner workings of the staff who have just lost an integral cog of the building. With the arrival of Cupp, it's clear she will not play by the average rules, immediately leading you to adoring the lovable bird-watching underdog. ​​​​​​​

A lesser appreciated crime drama, Untamed takes its audience to a beautiful local for a dark tale. Created by Mark L. Smith and Elle Smith, the thriller follows Kyle Turner (Eric Bana), a special agent for the National Parks Service, as he investigates the suspicious and brutal death of a woman who fell from El Capitan in Yosemite National Park. Partnered with Naya Vasquez (Lily Santiago), a rookie ranger who recently transferred from Los Angeles, Turner must navigate a lawless wilderness to uncover a deep-seated criminal conspiracy hiding beneath the park's scenic beauty. As the investigation intensifies, the dark secrets of the park force him to confront long-buried trauma from his own troubled past. A wonderful example of suspense and tension building, Untamed hooks you with the shocking event that sets the wheels in motion.

From the beginning, Untamed shows you the majestic locale the story is set in. The camera pans over Yosemite National Park before zooming in on the sheer rock face of El Capitan. Two rock climbers are scaling the cliff when a dead woman's body suddenly plummets from the summit above. The falling body crashes into their gear and gets tangled in their climbing ropes, violently tearing them off their anchors and putting both climbers' lives in immediate danger before they manage to stabilize themselves. It’s an utterly terrifying scene, and while your mind fears for the live ones, you rush to wonder why there is a dead body plummeting. With a breathtaking wilderness scale and immediate, visceral danger, the heart-stopping peril in a beautiful place reminds viewers that danger lurks anywhere. The sequence instantly forces the audience to wonder who the victim is, whether it is a murder or an accident, and how a body ended up at the top of one of the world's most famous rock formations.

OpenAI released a new cybersecurity model. Is that enough?

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Max Justicz, the chief technology officer and cofounder of the startup Charge Robotics, used an OpenAI model to find a severe vulnerability that — if exploited — could allow hackers to take control of servers running Ubuntu, a widely-used open-source Linux based operating system.

Justicz, who has a hobbyist’s interest in cybersecurity and previously found a vulnerability in the same codebase, told TechCrunch that he found this issue using GPT 5.6 Sol, released in July, through the AI maker’s Daybreak Blue, a program that gives cybersecurity defenders access to powerful frontier models.

“I literally just aimed ChatGPT at the same body of code and said ‘I found this bug previously that was fixed several years ago, please find bugs as bad or worse,’ and it came back in 2 hours with this,” Justicz said. “It’s extremely convoluted and hard for a human to find, but it works!”

He reported the issue to the Debian Project, which maintains the software in question and issued a patch to fix the vulnerability. There has been no known evidence of exploitation. Still, the ease with which he found it suggests that we’ve only seen the tip of the iceberg when it comes to potential exploits that even novice hackers can utilize with the help of AI models.

“I can only imagine what one could do with more time and access to less neutered models. We should expect some extremely crazy bugs to be found and exploited over the next few months,” Justicz wrote in a social media thread discussing his finding. “This might be obvious to most folks here, but there is currently huge alpha in taking some code or binary someone has not audited yet and just aiming a powerful AI model at it.”

OpenAI appears to have noticed. Today, the AI giant announced a new model focused on cybersecurity, ChatGPT Cyber, would be made available to the highest-tier of users in the cybersecurity program, Daybreak Red, which allows offensive cybersecurity researchers tools for vulnerability hunting and development. Yesterday, OpenAI said that the same model had been used to scan and prepare patches for vulnerabilities in the Linux kernel, finding 948 potential issues. OpenAI also found 81 “novel” vulnerabilities in Firefox, the web browser.

“Our goal is to dramatically expand Daybreak, bringing advanced security research to organizations of every size, independent researchers, and open-source maintainers,” the frontier lab said in a statement.

OpenAI has insisted that there is a narrow window of time for the people who safeguard digital infrastructure to take action before AI models become even better at hacking and easier to access. For cyber defenders, however, it can feel like the frontier labs have unleashed a mountain of new work that outstrips their resources. Justicz said that he personally spent about $5,000 on subscriptions and tokens to find several exploits.

Besides Daybreak’s model access, OpenAI has also announced a $1 billion initiative to support cybersecurity professionals in the program who work on critical infrastructure like the electrical grid or water systems.

The Daybreak program has its own critiques. Justicz notes that the frontier lab appears to have recently “nerfed” Daybreak Blue to make it less useful at finding vulnerabilities. One one hand, it may be good to limit access from potential bad actors — Justicz said he just uploaded a photo of his drivers’ license to join the program. On the other, cybersecurity professionals say they have one hand tied if they can’t use frontier technology while bad actors can deploy open-weight models with abliterated guardrails.

Still, OpenAI says just “thousands” of cybersecurity professionals are participating in the Daybreak program, versus a far larger number of potential hackers.

It’s unclear if frontier labs are devoting enough of their resources to this issue. Eric Wallace, a researcher on OpenAI’s technical staff, said that “we are now allocating a significant amount of our frontier compute towards discovering and patching vulnerabilities in open source codebases, fully pro bono.”

But the Linux bug hunt cost $1.2 million, which is significantly less than the approximate $6 million token spend the company invested in finding a potential solution to a famous problem of fluid dynamics.

If there is a narrow window of time to patch the digital infrastructure the world depends on, more urgency may be required.

macOS 27.3 Golden Gate public beta 1 now available, here’s everything new

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Following the release of macOS 27.3 developer beta 1, Apple is now rolling out the first test build for users enrolled in the public beta. Here’s what’s coming

Contrary to macOS 27.2, it looks like Apple had quite a bit in store for macOS 27.3 and its counterparts.

In addition to expanding Siri AI to four more languages, including Traditional Chinese, Norwegian, Turkish, and Vietnamese, the system also introduces several new Safari features, including Vertical Tabs, Adaptive Dark Mode for websites, and the ability to organize and manage tabs using Siri AI.

macOS 27.3 also adds native Markdown rendering to Quick Look, allowing users to preview .md files with proper formatting instead of raw Markdown syntax.

The update also expands Siri AI’s capabilities across Apple’s own apps, allowing users to perform more complex actions in apps like Messages, Music, Calendar, Photos, and Reminders, as well as manage files and folders.

Apple is also introducing new tools for third-party developers to create advanced Siri AI integrations using Markdown-based instructions, allowing the assistant to coordinate multistep actions across different apps.

Users who enjoyed the wallpaper introduced with the new touchscreen MacBook Pro will also find a new screensaver called Pro Lighteensaver, which features light moving along the metallic-looking lines that form the word “PRO.”

Earlier this week, Apple also confirmed that macOS 27.3 will introduce new checks for potentially harmful content shared through public iCloud links. Starting with the update, files and photos shared using the “Anyone with the link” option will be checked by Apple, which may remove flagged content or take action against accounts that violate its policies.

The release build for macOS 27.3 public beta 1 is 26C5035r, which is the same as the developer build.

If you’d like to join Apple’s free public beta program, visit beta.apple.com.

Did you spot anything new in today’s beta? Let us know in the comments.

Google’s first Wyoming data centre will run on gas with carbon capture

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The $17B Cheyenne campus leans on a 702 MW fuel cell plant, with emissions stored underground.

The entrance to Google’s office at Pancras Square in London.

Google will build its first Wyoming data centre, a $17B investment near Cheyenne. Most of its power will come from natural gas, including a 702 MW fuel cell plant from AEP and Bloom Energy, with carbon capture meant to store most emissions underground.

Google will build its first data centre in Wyoming, a long-term $17B investment in Laramie County, the company said on Thursday. Much of its power will come from natural gas, with carbon capture meant to keep most of the emissions out of the air. It follows a $15B plan for Malaysia that Google announced on Wednesday.

The main source is a 702 MW fuel cell plant from American Electric Power, which turns natural gas into electricity without burning it. AEP said in October it would spend about $4B on the Bloom Energy plant near Cheyenne. Google will buy its power under a 20-year deal, POWER Magazine reported, and the plant should be fully running by the end of 2028.

Carbon from the fuel cells will be captured and stored underground at Tallgrass Energy’s nearby Eastern Wyoming Sequestration Hub. Google says this will remove “a large majority” of the plant’s emissions, but has not given a figure. It calls the plant the largest of its kind planned so far.

Google will also buy power from gas plants run by Tallgrass and Black Hills Energy, and is studying whether carbon capture can be fitted to Tallgrass’s turbines. With Wyoming, its contracted power from plants with carbon capture reaches nearly 1 GW. Its first such deal, for a gas plant in Illinois, came in October 2025.

Google says it will pay for all the power it uses and the grid upgrades its growth requires, something US lawmakers want Big Tech to do. The data centre will be air-cooled to save water.

Water is a sore point in Cheyenne. In July, the city halted some data centre water discharges after a Meta contractor released a rare bacterium into its wastewater reuse system.

Google expects the project to bring in hundreds of millions of dollars in property taxes for the county and its schools. It and its partners are also setting up a community council, after more than 200 residents raised worries about how data centre growth is changing Cheyenne. Google announced the project two days after US voters rejected data centres in 40 of 41 local ballot measures.

Alina Maria Stan builds connections that people actually feel. Before Tekpon, Alina followed curiosity across industries and countries. Muse has a voice mode now

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Muse AI agent now has voices and ‘expressive’ avatars that animate while chatting with you.

Muse AI agent now has voices and ‘expressive’ avatars that animate while chatting with you.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Meta now lets you have voice conversations with your Muse AI agent in real time, CEO Mark Zuckerberg announced today.

The feature has been rolled out to me, and I gave it a spin on the iOS app while writing this article. As you chat with your Muse agent, the avatar at the top of your conversation animates in time with the words that it’s saying. You can also change the tone of the voice by just asking your agent about what you want and letting it decide or by browsing through a bunch of options in the avatar settings.

In my brief tests, my Muse agent responds quickly over voice, though it occasionally pauses while it’s looking up information or checking something for me (like it would with a text chat query). It also animates what it’s saying well, though for my particular avatar, it’s been a little uncanny. I set my Muse agent’s avatar to look like Mark Zuckerberg as a constant reminder that I’m chatting with a Meta service (I refuse to be charmed by a Jolly!), and seeing the digital Zuckerberg facsimile actually talk with me is quite weird. I also can’t get my Muse agent’s voice to sound exactly like Mark Zuckerberg.

Muse’s voice mode and expressive avatars are rolling out now in the US for the app, Meta says. They’re coming “soon” for Meta’s AI glasses and will also be available for its forthcoming Muse Charm gadget, which is supposed to ship this year.

Binance, Deribit revoke SignalPlus API keys after fake Teams invite attack

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SignalPlus said attackers compromised an engineer’s computer in a breach that resulted in losses of less than $82,000, which the crypto trading platform pledged to reimburse in full.

Exchanges Binance and Deribit said Thursday they had revoked API keys connected to crypto derivatives trading platform SignalPlus as a precaution following a security breach. Both exchanges said their own systems were unaffected, while Deribit warned that trading, monitoring and automation through SignalPlus had stopped working for its customers.

Binance co-founder Yi He also urged third-party platforms integrated with the exchange to review their security controls and monitor for suspicious activity, warning that the threat extended beyond any single company.

SignalPlus provides trading infrastructure for crypto derivatives, particularly options, allowing professional and institutional traders to manage positions across multiple exchanges through a single platform.

According to the company on X, the attack originated from a compromised Telegram account belonging to a contact at a third-party technology vendor. The attacker impersonated the contact and sent a fake Microsoft Teams meeting invitation to a SignalPlus engineer, compromising the employee’s computer.

The attacker remained dormant on the compromised computer before using the engineer’s access privileges to target SignalPlus’s internal network early Wednesday, the company said.

SignalPlus said it detected and contained the intrusion, with total losses of less than $82,000, and had begun fully reimbursing affected users. The company temporarily suspended its services and expects to restore operations over the next few days after completing security reviews and applying patches.

SignalPlus also acknowledged that it had not required IP whitelisting for exchange API access. “We take responsibility for this oversight,” the company said, adding that it would tighten API access requirements and strengthen internal security controls. IP whitelisting is a security measure that restricts API access to a predefined list of approved internet addresses.

Related: BitMart founder says security team ‘severely understaffed’ after new hack

The SignalPlus incident comes days after Binance disclosed details of a $6.3 billion hack on Oct. 29, which also involved compromised employee computers.

According to Binance’s postmortem, attackers posing as recruiters tricked an engineer into downloading a malicious file disguised as a coding assignment. The attackers subsequently used the compromised computer to gain access to additional systems and eventually breach the exchange’s wallet infrastructure.

Binance co-founder Yi He said Tuesday that the exchange had detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threats in 2026, including fraudulent job interviews, malicious software packages and attempts to steal employee credentials.

The Deals: Jared Gutstadt’s Audio Chateau Records Secures $9M Funding Round & More

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Other recent deals include a new tie-up between Oak View Group and Independent Artist Group and Adobe's licensing agreements with UMG and WMG.

Audio Chateau Records, a music and creator company founded by Jingle Punks’ Jared Gutstadt, secured up to $9 million in Series A financing to support its next phase of growth. According to a press release, Audio Chateau merges “the creative resources of a record label with the commercial capabilities of an agency and content studio,” recognizing “that artists are multifaceted creators and aims to help established and emerging talent grow their careers, brands and businesses.”

Investors in the funding round include Jonathan Schulman, Ben Lurie and Julie Gauthier of CW Ventures.

“I’ve seen the shift to creator culture firsthand, and I believe the next generation of successful artists will be the ones who build something bigger than their music,” said Gutstadt in a statement. “That’s what we’re building at Audio Chateau. We want to give artists the creative resources, relationships and opportunities to turn their talent into lasting businesses. This investment allows us to bring more talent into that model, strengthen what we’re building and create new avenues for how artists are discovered and developed.”

Oak View Group (OVG) partnered with Independent Artist Group (IAG) in a deal that will provide sponsorship and brand partnership opportunities to IAG’s roster. Through the agreement, touring and music assets from IAG artists will be made available to OVG brand partners through OVG’s network of sponsorship sellers in North America. The deal will encompass title sponsorships, artist presales, artist and brand engagements, fan activations, meet-and-greets and more.

Read on for more recent music industry deals.

Adobe announced it’s working with Universal Music Group (UMG) and Warner Music Group (WMG) to bring fully-licensed music clips directly to Adobe Premiere mobile. The deals will soon allow Adobe Premiere users to integrate up to 60-second music clips from UMG and WMG into their videos. The licenses will cover personal, non-commercial use only; users working on projects requiring commercially licensed music can continue to use Adobe Stock Audio. — Chris Eggertsen

U.K. indie ticketing platform Skiddle received its first external investment since its launch 25 years ago, with Soho Square Capital investing to “accelerate the company’s existing five-year growth plans,” according to a press release. Co-founders Ben Suborn and Richard Dyer will retain control of the business, with Soho Square taking a minority equity stake. Soho Square’s Joe Tebbutt will join Skiddle’s board.

The funding will help allow Skiddle to expand beyond music, festivals and nightlife; the company is looking to focus more on lifestyle events while also making additional investments in tech, marketing and business development. The Soho Capital investment will additionally allow Skiddle to expand into new European territories.

The deal with Soho Square is being touted as a greater partnership that will give Skiddle “access to additional strategic experience and a network of businesses that have previously navigated European expansion, supporting the company as it pursues its international plans with greater pace and scale,” according to the release.

Launched in 2001 by Suborn and Dyer, Skiddle generated more than 178 million pounds ($235 million) in face value ticket sales across 180,000 events in 2025, with revenue growing an average of more than 20% a year over the past two years, according to the company. — Chris Eggertsen

Music metadata infrastructure company Music Story signed a multi-year partnership with Sonos that will see the latter drawing on Music Story’s consistently updated music metadata — allowing Sonos devices to “better understand music: stronger discoverability, easy connection to external platforms, and fewer incorrect tracks played through the speakers,” according to a press release. — Chris Eggertsen

Sonilo, described in a press release as a “generative audio company building video-native music and sound models,” partnered with AI music attribution and rights management company Musical AI. According to the release, the companies “aim to demonstrate a model for generative music where licensing, attribution, and compensation are built into the process from the start.”

Sonilo “generates music and sound effects directly from video footage, text, or both, using models built around licensed source material,” the release adds. Through the deal, Musical AI’s technology will analyze Sonilo’s generated outputs to measure how licensed recording and compositions contributed to them. This will provide Sonilo’s licensing partners with the data they need to account for compensation for the songwriters, artists and other rights holders behind the original music.

Sonilo says it’s currently finalizing master recording and publishing agreements with major rights organizations as it readies the launch of its new music model.

England check on Palmer - what are Tuchel's other big choices?

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Thomas Tuchel will name his next England squad on Friday

England have made medical checks on Cole Palmer as Thomas Tuchel considers whether to select the Chelsea forward in his next squad.

The 24-year-old missed all four games during the previous international window with a minor injury.

He completed 90 minutes for Chelsea against Brentford before those matches, and started for the Blues in the first game afterwards.

Tuchel, who names his squad to face Croatia and Spain on Friday, said at the time that Palmer was "playing with discomfort and pain" but was "missing too many opportunities" with England.

Whether he is recalled will be a key theme on Friday. While it is normal for England medics to check on players under consideration, it also underlines that Palmer remains on Tuchel's radar despite his stark comments last month.

In addition to deciding whether to select Palmer, head coach Tuchel has a number of other dilemmas to resolve.

Bukayo Saka's injury absence opens the door to another attacker, with his Arsenal team-mate Noni Madueke pushing for a recall following a string of impressive performances.

Manchester United forward Marcus Rashford has not played since mid-October and is not fit for selection, while Chelsea defender Trevoh Chalobah is a major injury doubt.

Defenders Ben White and Djed Spence are pushing for recalls after missing out on the previous squad through injury.

The likes of Harry Kane, Jude Bellingham, Declan Rice and many others are now stonewall selections - but the intrigue is around the remaining few spots still seemingly up for grabs.

Will some old faces earn a recall or will there be new faces soon pulling on the England shirt? BBC Sport runs down some of the potential candidates for selection.

The 19-year-old Fulham midfielder has already been close to a senior-team debut.

King travelled to the US as part of the preparation squad before the World Cup, along with Alex Scott, Ethan Nwaneri and Rio Ngumoha.

Scott and Ngumoha have already made their debuts for the senior side and Nwaneri is currently on loan at Borussia Dortmund.

Tuchel said in his last press conference how King is close to a senior call-up and the midfielder has impressed for the under-21s too.

The biggest challenge for King will be finding a chance to shine in his favoured number 10 position with Bellingham being England's best player and the position being one of real strength for the Three Lions.

Will Lankshear has been prolific for Middlesbrough this season

There are a lack of number nines in the English game and the job of being Harry Kane's understudy is harder than it sounds.

The England captain has just won the Ballon d'Or, likes to play almost every minute for his country and is the captain, record goalscorer and arguably the best player in Tuchel's side.

Dominic Calvert-Lewin has been selected in the squads pre and post the World Cup but, despite doing well for Leeds, it has not quite clicked for him under Tuchel and he was given just 15 minutes in two substitute appearances in the last window.

Tuchel has started to use Bellingham as the back-up to Kane but he could opt for more of a prospect in his squad in this shorter window and turn to Lankshear.

The 21-year-old striker, who like Kane started at Tottenham before being sold to Middlesbrough this summer for Â£20m, has scored 15 goals in all competitions so far this campaign.

He has scored 13 goals in as many games in the Championship, helping Middlesbrough to the top of the division.

He only made his debut for the under-21s during the last international window and scored in that game so it would be quite a rise for a player who is not in the Premier League.

Tuchel is aware of Lankshear and with a lack of number nines across the age groups it could be an interesting option for a manager who is not afraid of making big calls.

He did miss Middlesbrough's loss to Wrexham on Wednesday with a tweaked hamstring but could return in time for Saturday.

The Everton number 10 has turned down call-ups to play for the Republic of Ireland as he focuses on his dream of trying to represent the Three Lions.

He has been one of the Toffees' standout performers having scored two goals and registered two assists in all competitions, helping Everton to seventh in the league.

Dewsbury-Hall, 28, is a clever technician with an eye for a pass and his ability on the ball would likely suit international football.

But team-mate James Garner, who Tuchel called a mini-Valverde, was called up before Dewsbury-Hall when there were dropouts in the last international break with that showing perhaps he has more to do to get on Tuchel's radar.

Morgan Gibbs-White was probably the unluckiest player to be left out of the initial squad last time around when Tuchel got his players together in September.

But the Nottingham Forest midfielder was called up as a late replacement after a number of high profile pull-outs and left a family holiday to join up with the Three Lions squad.

Tuchel values that kind of dedication and referenced the willingness of Gibbs-White, 26, to join up in the last camp.

The Forest captain featured during every game of the last break although he did not start a game.

But with Forest struggling at the bottom end of the table - and competing against the likes of Bellingham, Phil Foden, Palmer and Eberechi Eze - the midfielder could miss out again.

A must pick or unreliable? Tuchel's Palmer dilemma

White has been unlucky since he ended his international hiatus to return in March.

The Arsenal defender missed the World Cup with a knee injury that saw him also miss the Champions League final.

White then missed the first England squad of this campaign with injury, with Tuchel saying the 29-year-old would have been called up if he had been fit, citing his partnership with Saka as potentially good for the Three Lions.

But White has been available for all but one of Arsenal's games this season and would add a quality defensive option at right-back if selected, especially with Reece James missing through injury.

Jordan Henderson has not been in the England set-up since the World Cup, where he fell over an advertising hoarding following England's win over Mexico in the Azteca Stadium and broke his arm.

Many would have thought the 36-year-old's international career was over but Henderson, then at Brentford, made a move to Chelsea as they looked to add experience to their young squad.

After recovering from the arm injury, Henderson has surprised some to become an important part of the Chelsea midfield, starting five games in a row and scoring twice in a win over Bournemouth, before he missed the Blues' win over Manchester United with injury.

Henderson is a popular figure, respected by staff and players and is again performing at the top level.

Earth has captured a new mini moon  — but it won't stick around for long

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Stargazing tips, cosmic events and expert insights on the universe

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.

Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Earth has caught itself a new mini-moon, and it is sticking around for Christmas.

The space rock, designated 2026 QQ8, was first observed in August 2026 after entering orbit around Earth in April 2026. The discovery was reported by the International Astronomical Union's Minor Planet Center on Thursday (Oct. 29).

The stay of QQ8 is a temporary one. The mini-moon will remain in the thrall of Earth's gravity through December before breaking away in 2027.

Scientists have recreated the orbital path of QQ8, finding that it takes 359 days, or about 0.98 of an Earth year, to orbit the sun. QQ8 closely approaches Earth around once every ten years, but this time the object came so close that Earth has been able to gravitationally seize it.

According to the Minor Planet Center, the closest QQ8 will come to our planet during this stay is around 353,000 miles (568,000 kilometers). That is around 110,000 miles (177,000 km) further out than the average distance between Earth and the moon. Even if QQ8 were to come close enough to collide with Earth, it would likely be completely destroyed in our planet's atmosphere before it reaches the surface, as it is only around 6 feet (2 meters) in diameter.

This is far from the first time Earth has snagged itself a mini-moon, more accurately called a temporary satellite. In fact, research last year suggested that our planet could be orbited by as many as six mini-moons at any given time, with many of these being fragments of our more permanent natural satellite, the moon.

While QQ8 is tiny, it is larger than the average mini-moon. That is according to modelling earlier this year that suggested the average size for a temporary satellite is 75 centimeters (30 inches) across.

The same modelling suggested the average stay for such an object would be around nine months.

So if QQ8 was captured in April 2026, that means it should move away in January 2027.

The researchers behind those findings suggested that, were Earth to gravitationally grip a space rock around 10 feet (3 meters) wide, it could serve as a mini-moon for 10 to 20 years.

Robert Lea is a science journalist in the U.K. whose articles have been published in Physics World, New Scientist, Astronomy Magazine, All About Space, Newsweek and ZME Science. He also writes about science communication for Elsevier and the European Journal of Physics. Rob holds a bachelor of science degree in physics and astronomy from the U.K.’s Open University. Follow him on Twitter @sciencef1rst.

T-Mobile Taps Bryan Cranston in Bid to Wake Consumers Up to ‘T-Mix’ Plan

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

We are all about to get a very loud wake-up call from T-Mobile.

The telecommunications provider, one of the biggest and most colorful advertisers in the U.S., has dispatched actor Bryan Cranston in a new campaign that may surprise those who have grown familiar with its playful spots featuring celebrities such as Billy Bob Thornton, John Travolta, Zach Braff and Donald Faison. In the new spot, Cranston and a rooster named George aim to serve as an alarm clock of sorts, alerting consumers to “T-Mix,” a new wireless plan that lets consumers pick only the features they want ,and comes with perks. Related Stories MS NOW Has 2026 Midterm Elections Coverage: Here's How to Watch Election Night Results Live Online Free Fox News Sets Midterm Elections Coverage: Here's How to Stream 'Democracy 26' Online Free

“Come on sleepyheads, it’s up and at ‘em!” Cranston crows in the ad. “Because there’s been a change in plan!” Meanwhile, he sets about rousing people from hotel rooms, airplane seats and bedrooms.

In the commercial, “I represent all the people who need to hear this information,” says Cranston in a recent interview. “Because I was one of them until moments ago.”

The 60-second ad debuts during Amazon Prime Video’s “ Thursday Night Football” and will roll out across national broadcast, streaming, cinema, digital, social media platforms, as well as on radio and out-of-home, says Lucy McLellan, executive vice president and chief brand and communications officer at T-Mobile. And while T-Mobile is known for launching big campaigns, the company will also use digital to launch some bespoke creative that targets specific consumer niches.

The new work, created with a new agency of record, Highdive, will attempt “to reach new audiences who have tuned out the category completely,” says McLellan, and make an effort to “appeal to audiences beyond the millennials that T-Mobile has traditionally appealed to.”

Other telecommunications giants are also trying to reach out to bigger crowds. Verizon in June tapped actor Michael Meyers to reprise his “Austin Powers” character, and reconvene that film’s cast to tout a new “Simplicity Plan” that offered new promotions including a return on dollars spent as well as prizes and rewards.

Creating the high-energy pitchman wasn’t done in seconds. Cranston says the entire shoot took two days and that “we did each of those scenes several times from several different vantage points.” Telling a story via commercial is much more difficult than doing so in a TV series or movie, the actor says. “Every second is critical, whereas in a two-hour movie, well, you could let things linger,” he adds. “But this has to be cut to a millisecond in order to make the time requirements.”

Known best for roles like Walter White in “Breaking Bad,” Hal in “Malcom in the Middle” and Tim Whatley in “Seinfeld,” Cranston says he has long done commercials, which were “my life source” when he first started his acting career. Cranston has tackled ad assignments for CoffeeMate, for example, and is heard frequently as the voice of commercials for Ford Motor Co.

The character he plays for T-Mobile is “almost a Pied Piper,” he says. “I think my character was just another person who happened to have this epiphany that, wow, this is amazing. I can create my own plan. I don’t have to adhere to what was done, you know, decade after decade. And I can do this on my own,” he explains “I think that allowed me to be able to present it in an effusive way, in an energized way, without it being silly.”

George, the rooster who accompanies the actor throughout much of the ad, also maintained his demeanor. Relationships with animals — and, often, people too, says Cranston, are often “governed by food,” and the same was true of his fowl actor. “There was a little bowl of food on a stick that I can rattle, and here’s the rattle, and he would turn close to me. I was holding it, and I would hold it close, and then as soon as he turns toward me, I drop it out of frame. So the camera wouldn’t see it, and I tried to get closer and closer and closer to him, you know, just put my face right into his beak, you know, and have a stare down. And, you know, all you need is a couple seconds of that.”

And in a commercial, every second counts.

‘Executed for creating an AI poster for your burger restaurant’: Woke 3 visions captivate social media

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

According to social media, this week’s midterms are ushering in a new era: Woke 3.0.

For the uninitiated, the internet has broken down the dynamics of American politics in the 2020s into a few distinct eras. There was Woke 1.0 at the start of the decade, associated with far-left social movements of the time. As the country leaned toward conservatism in the mid-2020s, according to the internet, Democrats responded by moving into Woke 2.0, a shift that New York City Councilman Chi Ossé defined as “more productive” and less focused on virtue signaling than Woke 1.0.

Now, after the blue tsunami, social media is convinced that a third iteration of Woke might just be upon us. The newfound Democratic majorities in both chambers of Congress have social media anticipating a big cultural swing back to the left, and it’s got plenty of ideas of what that might look like. 

Welcome to Woke 3.0

As soon as Democrats started pulling away with the midterms, concepts for America under Woke 3.0 started going viral.

That includes everything from a rejection of AI (“Under Woke 3.0 you will be publicly executed for creating an AI poster for your burger restaurant,” one user wrote) to a celebration of trans athletes (“Woke 3 we’re having trans athletes swim in the Reflecting Pool,” another user quipped). 

“WOKE 3 IS HERE,” a third user declared. “EVERYONE JUST GOT ANOTHER SET OF PRONOUNS, YOU ARE NOW LEGALLY REQUIRED TO THEY/THEM YOUR DOG.”

One of the most prolific Woke 3.0 posters is an account known as Bald Ann Dowd, who imagined the blue wave bringing everything from universal healthcare “for diseases we don’t even have” to Ramadan-themed Hallmark movies. They even suggested we’re jumping straight to Woke One Billion: “We’re in the club sipping gluten free lean.”

But not everyone on social media was eager to “lib out.” Social media’s dreams of an ultra-progressive government depend on the politicians that are now in power, who may not be quite as eager to swing back to the far left.

One user pointed to a statement by Rep. Hakeem Jeffries (D-NY), who’s poised to become the next Speaker of the House. Jeffries told the press that he’s “ready, willing, and able to find bipartisan common ground” with Republicans, casting doubt on Congress’s willingness to embrace “Woke 3.0.”

“They’re already trying to compromise with a group that wants something diametrically opposed to them,” the user wrote. “Even with a majority they behave like controlled opposition; being feckless is all they know.”

The new millennial cringe

This summer, Rep. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez (D-NY) went viral for saying that “Woke 1 was crazy” on ABC News, granting the concept of different eras of “Woke” political legitimacy. AOC’s comment rubbed some people the wrong way, saying it was dismissive of the political work done in the early 2020s that continues into the modern day.

Now, AOC has sounded off on the wave of political optimism spurred by the midterms, saying it reminds her of how it felt when Barack Obama won the presidential election. In a video addressing Gen Z posted on Wednesday night, she encouraged younger Americans to embrace the “earnestness” of so-called “millennial cringe.”

“Imagine all the seats we flipped last night. That feeling, plus, on top of it, electing the first Black president ever. And on top of that, you did it the first time you ever voted,” AOC said. The now-37-year-old was just 19 when Obama was elected president in 2008.

“That hits generationally. Like, you are chasing that feeling ever since,” AOC continued. “And because you did it the first time you ever voted, you’re like, ‘Yeah, we can change the world. Why not? We did it the first time. Let’s just do it again.’”

“That’s so much of it in a nutshell,” she concluded. “Just keep at it.”

CNBC's experts on whether the AI trade is ending and what investors should do next

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Amid a powerful rout in tech stocks, investors told CNBC on Thursday that something may be fundamentally breaking in the AI trade following the big success of Democrats in Tuesday's midterm elections and the threat they try to slow the data center buildout.

Democrats are more aggressively anti-data center than Republicans, and Wall Street is quickly redoing its math what any restrictions they implement could mean for the related companies' future growth.

"Right now, investors are questioning that $1.2 trillion of capex that we forecast for next year," Peter Boockvar, chief investment officer at One Point BFG Wealth Partners, told CNBC. "Well, maybe that's not going to happen."

Boockvar believes this could be the start of the end of the AI trade as we know it.

Whether it's completely over or not, investors need to be reassessing how they're planning to play the AI trade going forward, the analysts said.

The Nasdaq Composite dropped 5% on Thursday and fell into correction territory, off by more than 11% from its record high set last month. The iShares Semiconductor ETF dropped 11% on Thursday, its biggest decline since during the Covid breakout in March 2020. The Global X Artificial Intelligence & Technology ETF (AIQ) is down 6% on the day and down 15% over the past month.

"There has been a huge change in the outlook for AI infrastructure buildout," Eric Johnston, head of equities at Cantor Fitzgerald, told CNBC. "The entire economy and the consumer and earnings growth are all levered to that buildout."

The selling Thursday spilled over to non-AI stocks with the Dow Jones Industrial average down more than 1,600 points.

Josh Brown pointed to the 4.8% yield for the 10-year Treasury yield, saying a compelling risk-free rate is causing investors to reconsider taking the risk in these volatile AI stocks.

"You don't need to be at 80% U.S. stocks and have 60% of that exposure be tech," Brown said.

Many investors believe the AI trade isn't over completely, investors just need to get more selective.

"The AI buildout just got harder," said Rob Sechan, Newedge Capital Group Founder & Managing Partner:

"Nobody voted to make compute less scarce," Sechan said, citing Microsoft's Azure cloud business growing 48% year over year in its most recent third quarter. That level of growth suggests its capacity constrained, not demand constrained, he said.

"When new capacity gets harder to build, existing capacity becomes more valuable," Sechan said.

Richard Clode, portfolio manager at Janus Henderson, noted a few bright spots in tech on Thursday despite the highly correlated downward movements in the big tech indices.

"Motorola Solutions had some good results," he said. "We're seeing a little bit of rotation into some of the … internet names...Sony reported some good results, and again they were up strongly overnight."

"So there are some brief pockets of respite from this, but obviously pretty painful across the board today," he said.

Some experts believe the selling was overdone and investors could start nibbling.

This "indiscriminate selling" is a "buying opportunity," said Larry Adam, Raymond James chief investment officer. If investors "take a longer term perspective, which is what we do, I think the valuations at today's levels will tend to prove to be a good buying opportunity," he said, urging investors to look at the fundamentals.

Victoria Greene, chief investment officer of G Squared Private Wealth, said she saw some strong supports around the S&P 500's 200-day moving average and was on the lookout for a market bounce following Thursday's rout.

"Typically, the average return is plus about 180 basis points the next day," she said. "The question we have to ask ourselves: Is this a pullback in an uptrend, which is what I believe? Or is this the end-all, be-all [and] the wheels are coming off and everybody just needs to buy T-bills? I don't think we're there yet."

Google, Meta and TikTok are all suing California to block social media law intended to help kids

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Assembly Bill 1709 demands that platforms remove "psychologically exploitative features."

Google, Meta and TikTok are suing to block a recent California law that's intended to protect kids from some of the more harmful aspects of social media, according to Reuters. Assembly Bill 1709 is part of a larger package that Governor Gavin Newsom introduced earlier this year and it prohibits social media platforms from offering underage kids "psychologically exploitative features" that could lead to compulsive use.

This includes stuff like sending videos through autoplay and giving children under 16 personalized recommendations. The three social media giants say this is in violation of the First Amendment and Section 230 of the federal Communications Decency Act, which has often been used to shield tech companies from liability.

The three companies filed separate suits at various courts throughout California. "The state cannot, consistent with the First Amendment, override Meta's editorial judgments," Meta wrote in its complaint. Google and TikTok made similar arguments.

Newsom said when he signed the law that children's safety should always come first when considering social media platforms in California. The state "will not stand by while unregulated technology puts our children at risk," he noted.

Some organizations agree with the social media companies here. The nonprofit Electronic Frontier Foundation has called the law "a massive privacy and free speech nightmare" and unsuccessfully urged Newsom to veto the policy.

While many disagree on the potential solution, there's not much of an argument to be made regarding the severity of the problem. TikTok, Meta and Google are currently fighting thousands of lawsuits by individuals, school districts, cities and government entities that all claim that social media platforms addict children and have caused a mental health crisis. To that end, many states regulate children's access to social media.

Also, Meta recently agreed to pay up to $18 billion to settle a high-profile case over children's privacy and social media addiction. As part of this agreement, the company says it will make changes to its platforms to make them less addictive to young users. It still denies any wrongdoing.

Today's lawsuits are still in the early stages. We'll update you as they make their way through the California court system.

Iowa elected a Democrat whod dropped out of the U.S. midterms, and the internet has jokes

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The 2026 U.S. midterm elections delivered a decisive Democrat victory this week, with a blue wave taking control of both the Senate and the House of Representatives. It also unexpectedly swept up a Democratic candidate from Iowa who'd dropped out of the race, putting him in office regardless — and prompting numerous jokes online.

In September, Democratic nominee Eric Armstrong suspended his campaign for Iowa House District 20 due to health issues.

"Our District deserves a representative who can give 100 percent of their energy to this race," Armstrong wrote in a Sept. 7 Facebook post announcing his decision. "Right now, I must focus on healing so that I can continue to serve our community in other ways in the future."

With Armstrong no longer campaigning, Republican Keith Jones appeared to have a much clearer path to victory. But Armstrong's announcement came after Iowa's deadline for nominees to withdraw, meaning his name remained on the ballot when voters headed to the polls on Nov. 3.

As such, Iowans who took to the polls apparently looked at his announcement, looked at the ballot, looked back at Armstrong's withdrawal, and basically said, "Nah, we're not accepting that."

Despite having stopped campaigning in September, Armstrong defeated Jones with 55.2 percent of the vote, keeping the seat in Democratic hands. He will succeed fellow Democrat Josh Turek, who flipped a Republican-held U.S. Senate seat in Iowa.

The unexpected result has inspired jokes online imagining Iowa voters as particularly demanding bosses, unwilling to approve Armstrong's request for time off.

"Iowa said 'you are still going to work,'" wrote TikTok user @jalasherese. "We don’t care if we have to wheel you in there."

"Drink some ginger ale and we'll see you in January Eric," @mona_licia83 commented on TikTok.

For his part, Armstrong has indicated that he intends to serve, although his health remains a concern. In an interview with the New York Times, Armstrong said that he was still dealing with health issues but added, "I'm going to do my best to represent [the district]."

According to his campaign website, his priorities include expanding access to pre-K childcare, closing the minimum wage gap between Iowa and Nebraska, and improving benefits for Iowa educators to help schools recruit and retain staff.

I Got an Early Look at Amazon's New Echo Devices. Here's What to Know.

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Your smart home just got even... smarter.

Gear-obsessed editors choose every product we review. We may earn commission if you buy from a link. Why Trust Us?

Amazon just introduced a new lineup of Echo devices, and there's a lot more going on here than your typical smart speaker upgrade. The latest releases include everything from a smart alarm clock and an AI-powered photo frame to a new Echo Dot and a massive 16-inch smart display, all designed to make everyday life a little easier with Alexa+.

I recently got an early look at these devices during a private media briefing with Amazon ahead of their release, and I was genuinely impressed by what they could do in action. What stood out to me was how each product serves a different purpose, especially the new Echo Studio, which Panos Panay—Senior Vice President of Amazon Devices—used to spontaneously call his parents. The device was connected to a TV, and his parents, located in his childhood home, had the same device also connected to a TV. It was basically a video call for all of us media to see, and the interaction was so sweet. It also helped me realize just how advanced these Amazon Devices have become (and also how convenient!).

Whether you're looking to upgrade your bedside setup, run your smart home more smoothly, or find a great tech gift, here's a breakdown of four of Amazon's newest devices and who they're best suited for.

If you reach for your phone the second your alarm goes off, the new Echo Time might convince you to break that habit. Amazon's first smart alarm clock built for Alexa+ features a 3.2-inch touchscreen that displays your alarms, weather, and music, plus hundreds of customizable clock faces. It also has a sunrise-style wake-up light that gradually brightens in the morning, along with optional sleep-sensing technology that can wake you during lighter sleep.

At $199.99, it's a solid option for anyone looking to improve their sleep routine without adding another distracting screen to their nightstand. It comes in Graphite, Glacier White, and Coral Pink, with a separate kids' version available, too.

Digital photo frames are nothing new, but Amazon's Alexa Photo Frame takes it further with built-in Alexa+ features. Its 10-inch HD touchscreen has an anti-glare, semi-matte finish that makes pictures look more like actual framed photographs, while light-sensing technology automatically adjusts the brightness throughout the day.

The coolest part is how you interact with your photos. You can ask Alexa to pull up pictures from a specific trip or memory, add voice notes to individual photos, and even have family members contribute new pictures remotely. Starting at $199.99, it's a thoughtful gift for parents or grandparents who want to keep up with family memories without scrolling through their phones.

Amazon's newest Echo Dot packs several upgrades into its compact design. Along with fuller sound and 50 percent more bass than the previous generation, it now includes a built-in smart home hub, letting you connect and control compatible lights, locks, thermostats, and other devices without buying a separate hub. It also includes a built-in eero Wi-Fi extender that can expand coverage if you already have a compatible eero network.

Starting at $99.99, it's a great entry point for anyone who wants to make their home smarter without investing in a larger display. Families should also check out the Amazon Kids Echo Dot, which starts at $109.99 and comes in adorable Octopus and Penguin designs, plus a year of Amazon Kids+ with kid-friendly audiobooks, music, and podcasts.

If you want something that does a little bit of everything, the new Echo Show 16 is probably the most versatile device in Amazon's latest lineup. The redesigned 16-inch full HD display can function as a family calendar, smart home dashboard, digital photo frame, or even a TV, thanks to built-in Fire TV. You can use it to keep track of appointments, manage compatible smart devices, follow recipes in the kitchen, or stream your favorite shows.

It's especially useful for families who want one central place to manage what's happening around the house. The standard display starts at $499.99, while the $569.99 Nanomatte version offers an upgraded anti-glare screen. Both are available for preorder, with shipping expected later this year.

Amazon's new Echo Studio with Home Camera takes its premium smart speaker a step further by adding a built-in camera that lets Alexa+ actually see what's happening around it. That means you can hold up a board game and ask Alexa to explain the rules, or use the Alexa app to check on your pets while you're away. On the audio side, it still delivers powerful bass and immersive Dolby Atmos sound, with technology that automatically adjusts the audio to your room's acoustics.

At $249.99, it's a great option for anyone who wants high-quality sound with some pretty impressive smart home features. It comes in Graphite, and if you're not sold on having a camera in your speaker, Amazon still offers the standard Echo Studio for $219.99.
