Alex Karpovsky on Returning to Filmmaking (and Maybe ‘Girls’) After More Than a Decade Away

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The actor and director is making the indie festival rounds with his wild jungle comedy ‘Riptides,’ while also reflecting on his ‘Girls’ experience ahead of the revival: “Will I be in it? Who knows!”

Even if Alex Karpovsky was no more familiar to Girls viewers than the rest of its breakout cast, the Massachusetts native was already relatively established by the time of the HBO show’s premiere — only, behind the camera. He’d directed three micro-budgeted features from 2006 to 2009, and another two that released in 2012, the same year as Girls’ first-season run. The work showcased an idiosyncratic filmmaker freely following his heady, sometimes absurdist instincts.

Then Girls became what it became, and Karpovsky suddenly had an acting career coming off of his deadpan turn as Ray Ploshansky: Now 51, he’s gone on to star in Homecoming, Nobody Wants This, Angelyne and multiple Coen brothers movies. He’d tried to get other projects off the ground over the last decade-plus, including several TV ideas that got stuck in limbo, but he also acknowledges that time was an issue — he simply didn’t have the “mojo” to get another movie going. Related Stories TV Sharon Horgan Comedy 'Youth' Renewed for Second Season at HBO TV 'The Birds' Series With Sarah Snook in the Works at HBO

It feels fitting, then, that Karpovsky returned with his first film in 14 years less than two weeks before Lena Dunham’s announcement that Girls would be coming back as a five-episode limited-series revival. Karpovsky hasn’t been announced to be part of it — he won’t confirm or deny in our interview, but he’s open about imagining Ray’s future and his experience making the show — but there’s a full-circle quality to this arc nonetheless. The world, and the industry, have changed quite a bit, but Girls is back, and Karpovsky is back making movies.

The film in question is Riptides, and it is again very low-budget, playful with its humor and dense with ideas. Set in Sayulita, Mexico, where Karpovsky visits regularly, the movie imagines the collision between Toby (Steve Little), an American beach bum who oversees hotel properties in the idyllic town; Paul (Dylan Baker), a renowned self-help author sneaking in a quick vacation before his next big book tour; and Paul’s daughter, Michelle (Bojana Novakovic), an even-keeled manager who spends most of her time keeping her famous father out of trouble.

Tragedy strikes, and an opportunity arises for Toby to make something of himself and find some meaning, inspired by Paul’s writings. He winds up entangled with Michelle in this scheme, going down a “demented” path as Karpovsky puts it in our conversation.

Riptides next screens at the Indie Memphis Festival on Saturday evening. So far, its path is not dissimilar from the low-fi projects that Karpovsky helmed back in the late aughts and early 2010s, despite drastic changes in the surrounding independent landscape. We get into all of that and more.

You premiered about a month ago at New/Next Film Festival. How did it go?

It went really well. I mean, for me and my disposition, it’s terrifying to show this. The theater had 300 people in it. I know six people in the theater. It’s mostly strangers and you just never know how it’s going to go. Perhaps this is obvious, but the thing about a comedy is that everyone knows in real time whether or not the movie is working or not working — a binary thumbs up, thumbs down feeling — at least for me. You’re in the room and then you have to go up there and do a Q&A. But luckily everything played as I wanted it to…. One little test you can have of how a movie plays at film festivals: Do people stay for the Q&A, quite simply? And they did, and they asked good questions. So I was very happy — perhaps, more than happy, relieved that the movie didn’t fall apart.

It’s been 14 years since your last feature came out, the same year that Girls premiered — so the timing there is interesting. What do you attribute the gap to?

I made five movies before this one and they were all really ultra, ultra low-budget affairs. Two of them came out in the same year — 2012, I guess, Rubberneck and Red Flag — and we were in the middle of shooting Girls. It was fun and exhilarating, but really exhausting. Making any movie is exhausting. But making ultra low-budget movies, because you’re wearing so many hats, it exhausts you in a very specific way. I felt very depleted and I didn’t really have the desire to make another movie, at least a low-budget movie, for a while.

I also then got caught up in TV projects. I sold or was adjacent to sales for six TV shows; some of them got close, one of them aired, but it didn’t open a new chapter of my life as much as I wanted it to. But it took a decade of my life developing these six TV shows and shooting some of them and writing scripts for other ones. I thought about, “Well, I have four months here, maybe I can write a script and then shoot it in my next break.” But I just quite honestly didn’t really have the mojo. I didn’t have the drive to make another movie. And yes, I also started acting more after Girls than I’d ever acted before. But about two years ago, I had more free time and, more importantly, a resurrected enthusiasm to make another movie.

The industry has gone through so much since 2012. What felt different about making this scale of movie now versus then?

I expected to feel more of a change than I actually did, to be honest with you. The biggest changes that I experienced were technical. The cameras are a lot better and cheaper. The lights are a lot better and cheaper. The editing software’s a lot better and cheaper. So it’s easier in a sense, financially and technically, to make an ultra low-budget movie that looks decent. When I shot my last movies, we were using these Canon DSLR cameras, which were all the rage back then, but you can’t really do handheld stuff — there were a lot of limitations to it. Now for almost the same price, you can have a camera make an image that I think most civilians couldn’t tell whether it’s Hollywood or the camera you got at Best Buy for $3,000.

Did you consider scaling up, budget-wise, in your return to filmmaking?

I couldn’t get the money to scale up, so I did it this way — very affordably. My approach over the past few years has been to write something that would require funds and resources and patience. While that is hopefully incubating somewhere and getting slow momentum somewhere behind the Hollywood curtain, you’re not waiting for the phone to ring. You’re keeping busy making smaller projects that you can actually control and have agency over. It’s also the approach a lot of my friends and I’m still waiting for some of these other projects to maybe have an opening, but I can’t just hope that that’ll happen. I have to stay busy.

I want to go back to what you asked a moment ago.

Now that I think about it, although I don’t know how conscious of it I was when I was shooting it: The world has become more spicy. I don’t know if that’s the adjective, but there’s a lot more anger, it seems like, in the world than there was 14 years ago. A lot more people seem to take a thrill in criticizing you. There’s a lot of haters, you can say. There were haters around back in the day, but they didn’t seem to be in these numbers or have this type of volatility to them. So back in the day, I think it required less courage to make movies. Now, in addition to everything else it requires to make a movie, you also just have to have the fortitude to endure the arrows that will invariably come your way. I feel like there are more arrows than there used to be.

Even with something as simple as the advent of Letterboxd, the feedback is also more immediate in a way that I imagine is probably terrifying.

Yeah, it’s absolutely terrifying. There’s so much fear involved in making a movie to begin with, and then you add this other very powerful and visceral layer on top, it stops a lot of people from expressing themselves, I think.

Your film is in conversation with this topic to some degree — you play around with the Instagram interface as a way of introducing characters, and the power of an influencer’s support makes for a key plot point. It’s not the most obvious theme for this story, so how did you find your way in there?

Social media and social media influencers are now woven into our new economy. The way that people share and present and market ideas and products in the world — social media influencers now are a huge factor in that process. I wanted the movie to feel relevant in certain ways. Since COVID, there’s been a real surge in self-help interests. They’ve spiked upwards and I don’t know all the answers, but I’m curious why that’s going on. And a lot of people are now making their money through social media influence. I read somewhere that they asked 8-year-old kids, “What do you want to be when they grow up?” — the question they’ve asked for generations — and now it’s like 40 percent?

What else inspired the film? It’s set in Sayulita, Mexico, for starters.

We shot in Sayulita, which is a small town. It’s an hour north of Puerto Vallarta. I’ve been going there for about 10 or 15 years because it’s easy to get to and it’s so beautiful. I started to meet people down there. There’s a lot of stoners and burnouts and yoga people and surf people and local people. When I was talking to the expats and gringos that are down there, they’d spend their days surfing and smoking weed and drinking with their pals. In moments of stress, which is often when I visited, I was filled with a sense of envy for these people because they seemed to be really enjoying their life and they seemed genuinely happy. It wasn’t a front for most people. And then there were other times where I would talk to some of these people and I felt judgy, like I pitied them.

I thought, “Well, these people feel like they’ve abandoned something. They’ve surrendered a dream, they’ve surrendered their ambition, and there’s something sad in that too.” I kept oscillating, and that became an interesting subject for me: Happiness versus meaning is ultimately what it could be crystallized down to. And I wanted to Trojan horse this idea into an absurd, silly jungle comedy.

Can you talk about finding the tone in terms of the casting? Steve is very broadly funny, while Bojana is a grounding force. How did you develop that so it’d feel complementary?

I really wanted to make sure we nailed the tone, and I wanted to make sure everyone was acting in the same movie… When you’re mixing absurd comedy with something kind of grounded, and there’s death involved, and there’s darkness involved, I just wanted to make sure those things can blend together — and if they do blend together, make sure that everyone knows how they blend together and know how to plug into the same movie together. That really starts with the actors, and I feel like, exactly as you said it, Steve really captured the comedy of it, but also the heart of it and the tenderness of it and the innocence of it. Bojana really captured the drama and the tragedy of it.

I didn’t really know if it would work until they started having scenes together. When we shot the movie, I wanted to make sure we shot some of their pivotal interaction scenes early on to establish the movie, so everyone could see the movie we were making tonally.

Going forward, do you hope to work at a more accelerated pace behind the camera now? Does it feel sustainable to you to keep making movies at this scale, given the current mechanisms for distribution and indie financing?

I would like to keep making movies in an accelerated way. What’s really nice about making low-budget movies, to say the obvious, is that you have a lot of control. I don’t have to answer to anyone, for better or for worse. If I want to cast this person, I can cast them. If I want to shoot in this location, I can do so. If I want to make these choices in the editing room, I can do so. You also don’t need to wait very long, relatively speaking, to make these low-budget movies. You don’t need to wait four years to package and then hope that no one drops out at the last minute.

I’m still hoping one of my bigger projects might catch momentum out there in Hollywoodland. It’d be a fun and new adventure to embark upon, but I can’t just wait and do positive visualization exercises all day and hope that that’ll happen. I have to take control of my own life. Also, I should say it’s fun. I had a tremendous amount of fun making this movie, and to me, that’s one of, if not the main goal, to be creative with friends, doing something that you are hopefully proud of in places that hopefully are fun to shoot in.

Let’s talk about the Girls revival. All very exciting. Are you in? What are you hearing?

I hear they’re making it. I hear it’s happening. Will I be in it? Who knows!

I’m very curious what he’s been up to. It’s been 10 or 15 years, so I’m very curious what he’s been up to as the world has changed so much since we last saw all these people. I love Ray and I care for him deeply, and sometimes I’m concerned about him, and I hope he’s doing well. I hope he’s found meaning in his life. He seemed a little bit existentially adrift at times to me, or existentially disoriented. That allowed for him to view things maybe a little bit more clearly than other people, but it also has created an inner turmoil. I hope he’s less tormented than he has been, but maybe he won’t be as interesting if he’s less tormented. Mixed feelings!

You were talking earlier about the explosion in haters online, which made me wonder: Did you read Lena Dunham’s memoir?

I didn’t, no. And I don’t know if I have a good reason for it. All of my friends read it, and they all gave me their own synopses and also their own descriptions of when I was mentioned — which, from what I understand, was very sparing, contained. So I haven’t read it. One day I will.

She’s very open about what she went through on that show, including all that negative feedback.

I can tell you this, David. When we made the show, they were very happy years in my life, those six or seven years. I loved playing Ray, and I loved being in a show that people were talking about. The vibe on set, from my very limited point of view, was really positive and fun. I’ve been on some sets before and afterwards; invariably, there’s tension that brews. It just happens, especially the longer you spend time with people. I just didn’t see too much of it from my very limited perspective, but I’m an actor on the show. I’m not privy to a lot of these conversations.

Lena was just incredibly open and receptive. The vibe on set almost always trickles down from the leader. Lena was definitely our leader, and so the vibe that she at least presented when I was there was just very positive, enthused, and receptive. That shaped a lot of people’s feelings on set. It shaped my feelings on set. I’m getting around to saying that my time during those six years was very positive — and maybe the reason why I haven’t read the book yet is because I don’t want anything to change that. I heard there are things that might change that, so I kind of want to stay in my happy place.

I won't lie, this whole ethical non-monogamy is beginning to give me a headache.

A new clip from the upcoming episode is here, thanks to Entertainment Tonight, and I'm learning (before streaming with an HBO Max subscription) that Andi had two lovers before coming to Australia. 90 Day Fiancé's Angela Deem Drops Cryptic Message About Michael When Asked About Hunt For Love

Could things turn around before the end of the season?

Bitcoin crashes below $76,000 as AI bubble fears wipe $2.5 trillion from Wall Street

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A violent selloff in technology stocks spilled into crypto, triggering $1 billion in liquidations as investors questioned the sustainability of the AI boom.

Technology stocks plunged after the Democratic midterm sweep intensified concerns over AI spending, dragging Bitcoin lower as leveraged traders suffered heavy losses.

Bitcoin hit an intraday low of $75,568 on Thursday and recovered to $76,830.67 as of press time, as a historic selloff in US technology stocks spilled into crypto markets and triggered more than $1 billion in liquidations across leveraged trading positions.

According to CoinGlass data, approximately $1 billion in leveraged positions had been liquidated over the previous 24 hours, affecting more than 201,000 traders.

Bullish positions accounted for $926.8 million, or nearly 89% of the total, compared with $118.2 million in bearish bets.

Bitcoin accounted for $264.2 million in liquidations, followed by Ethereum at $165.3 million and Solana at $33.5 million. The largest single liquidation was a $10.94 million ETH long position on Binance.

Other is CoinGlass’s displayed grouped category, not a calculated residual. The list is incomplete and must not be summed as the market-wide total. The heatmap includes equity-linked labels such as SOXL, SNDK and XYZ:MU, not only cryptocurrencies. Exact snapshot time unavailable; figures are shown at screenshot precision and could not be cross-checked against a historical API snapshot.

Source: CoinGlass liquidation dashboard — user-provided Nov. 5 screenshot

The losses coincided with a sharp reversal on Wall Street, where the Nasdaq Composite plunged 4.9% and entered correction territory, falling more than 10% below its October record. The S&P 500 declined 3.2%, while the Dow Jones Industrial Average shed more than 1,100 points.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The Kobeissi Letter estimated that approximately $2.5 trillion had been wiped from US stock market valuations and reported that the Cboe Volatility Index, Wall Street's closely watched fear gauge, had surged more than 70%.

The market commentary firm attributed the speed of the collapse to excessive leverage meeting an abrupt change in investor sentiment, rather than a sudden deterioration in corporate fundamentals.

The selloff followed the Nov. 4 rally, when investors initially welcomed the Democrats' sweeping midterm victories as a potential constraint on President Donald Trump's economic policies.

However, attention quickly shifted toward the implications for the artificial intelligence industry, whose enormous capital expenditure commitments have helped sustain US equity valuations.

Investors are increasingly concerned that tighter political oversight, rising construction costs, and local opposition to data centers could undermine the expected growth in AI infrastructure spending.

“Such a ban, if even possible to enforce, would effectively mean the US surrendering its position in the global AI race to China.”

Those concerns have gained traction following Tuesday's elections. According to Ballotpedia, 46 of the 47 local data-center-related measures decided across the US in 2026 favored tighter restrictions, although some votes were advisory rather than legally binding.

The political backlash threatens to complicate an investment cycle built on expectations of continued demand for AI chips, electricity, computing infrastructure and data-center construction.

Nvidia fell below $200 for the first time since August, while the Nasdaq 100 declined approximately 5.5%, putting it on course for its steepest daily loss since April 2025.

Economist Mohamed El-Erian questioned whether the rest of the US economy could maintain its momentum if technology investment slowed.

He also cautioned against assuming that Democratic control of Congress would necessarily translate into a substantial slowdown in the technology sector.

Meanwhile, Citrini Research argued that expectations for AI infrastructure spending may have peaked as investors reassess whether projected returns can justify the industry's enormous capital requirements.

The research firm clarified that it was identifying a potential peak in spending estimates.

The distinction is significant for Bitcoin, which has increasingly traded alongside technology stocks during periods of financial market stress.

Thursday's simultaneous collapse in technology shares and cryptocurrency prices suggests investors are reducing exposure to speculative assets as uncertainty over AI investment returns, financing costs, and future regulation intensifies.

The next test comes as markets assess whether the political backlash will materially slow data-center construction or merely reduce expectations for how quickly the AI industry can expand.

UK weighs ban on Meta Ray-Bans and other smart glasses, citing 'secret filming'

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The United Kingdom is joining a growing list of countries looking to potentially restrict or outright ban smart glasses, especially in public spaces. Satvir Kaur, the country's Safeguarding minister, said in a Guardian report, "The government is looking into options to restrict smart glasses in certain settings and restricting the sale to certain users."

Kaur added that the benefits of smart glasses have to be weighed against their harms. In England and Wales, Meta glasses were recently banned in courtrooms, much like a similar ban in New York state. The courts promised to confiscate the glasses if they were worn into the courtroom (and returned after upon leaving).

According to Kaur, England's National Health Service and Department for Education are separately looking into policies regarding smart glasses.

In the last few months, smart glasses, like the Ray-Ban Meta, have faced a public backlash, even earning the moniker "pervert glasses."

In a debate about the glasses, MP Anne Sabina said, "Women are reporting waking up after nights out to find videos of themselves have gone viral online, filmed without their knowledge or consent by men wearing smart glasses."

Whether or not a ban is enacted, Britain is adding to a growing chorus of governments and agencies looking at restricting smart glasses.

Australia's government announced in September that it was barring the use of camera-equipped glasses in government workspaces. The EU is scrutinizing glasses, though nothing official has been pursued yet.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

Norway is reportedly planning a temporary ban on smart glasses in public spaces

In the United States, even ICE has banned Ray-Ban Meta glasses for its agents, citing privacy and security concerns. With the recent "blue wave" in the 2026 midterm elections, Silicon Valley and AI are expected to face increased scrutiny. More than likely, smart glasses will be included in that backlash.

Even Kaur pointed out that built-in cameras and displays could be beneficial for visually impaired individuals. "Smart glasses have been a game-changer, helping them navigate the world around them," Kaur said.

However, smart glasses don't necessarily need cameras to be beneficial.

Meta claims it was working on its camera-free Ray-Ban Meta Audio for years before their debut at Meta Connect 2026. Perhaps the company was, but at the time it seemed to launch in time to combat some of the pervert glasses criticisms.

Several Tom's Guide writers have tried the Audio-only Meta glasses, and we've all come away with the same praise: it was the right call.

"Taking out the camera has made me an even bigger fan of Ray Ban Meta glasses," AI editor Amanda Caswell said after 72 hours with the glasses.

There is a way for smart glasses to gain traction, and perhaps ditching the camera could help.

What are your thoughts? Should smart glasses with cameras be banned, or do they have their uses?

Esther McGregor is officially our Violet Sorrengail, and in the wake of that news, she’s making a splash on the red carpet. However, while the star of the upcoming Fourth Wing series looks incredible in her latest outfit, I have to say, it is giving more Hunger Games than Empyrean in the best way.

However, it is giving Hunger Games, which is very fun, and it serves as a good reminder to go see McGregor’s pal in Sunrise on the Reaping while we wait to see her take on the role of Violet Sorrengail in Fourth Wing.

US government warns of ‘ongoing cyber threat’ amid mass hacks of industrial retail and military freezers

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

The U.S. government is warning of an “ongoing cyber threat” facing industrial refrigeration units, after reports of cyberattacks targeting freezers and fridges in supermarkets around the world, U.S. military bases, and at least one blood bank.

U.S. cybersecurity agency CISA, the FBI, and several other federal government departments warned in a joint statement on Thursday that hackers are targeting and compromising internet-connected refrigeration and cold chain monitoring systems in grocery stores and cold storage facilities. The statement said that the hacks are hitting critical infrastructure, such as the food, agriculture, transportation, and medicine-shipping industries.

“If successfully exploited, the actors could change the temperature and possibly cause physical impacts such as fire hazards, loss of inventory and changes to the service life of the system,” the agencies warn.

The warning comes after several waves of cyberattacks targeting industrial refrigeration units across the United Kingdom and Europe in recent weeks. Two separate waves of attacks allowed hackers to crank up the temperature on freezers and chilling units in at least a dozen countries, spoiling defrosted food and destroying produce. Some of the affected systems included remote-controlled refrigeration units made by Danish company Danfoss, which had stopped providing security updates to its customers’ freezers.

Hackers also targeted a blood bank in France, causing its refrigerated units to boost temperatures in its cold units containing donated blood supplies — but were stopped before the attack could compromise blood stores. The U.S. Department of Defense is investigating a spate of hacks targeting freezers run by the military, according to Reuters. The hacks were first reported by Signal and Silence.

The U.S. has not attributed the hacks to a particular group or government, but security researchers have broadly attributed the attacks to Iran; U.S. officials also reportedly suspect Iran is the likely culprit.

CISA, the FBI, and the Departments of Agriculture, Health and Human Services, and Transportation urged affected industries to remove remote control systems from the internet, change and strengthen passwords, apply patches where possible, and retire equipment that’s no longer supported by the vendor.

In a separate fact sheet, the departments said that hackers may attempt to “force units into continuous heating and defrost cycles, which can cause widespread inventory loss and equipment hazards.”

“Every organization that depends on refrigeration and monitoring systems should assume it could be a target,” said CISA’s acting cybersecurity chief Chris Butera.

Hyundai is recalling nearly 30,000 Kona Electric vehicles due to an airbag software defect

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Hyundai issued a recall affecting nearly 30,000 Kona Electric vehicles due to a software error that could cause the airbag to fail to deploy in a crash.

In a letter sent to the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration on November 4, Hyundai said it was recalling certain 2019 to 2023 Kona Electric and 2019 to 2022 Veloster N vehicles.

The recall impacts 37,901 vehicles in total, including 29,058 Kona Electric and 8,843 Veloster N models.

According to documents submitted to the NHTSA, the issue stems from a software error with the airbag control unit (ACU) that can cause the airbag malfunction indicator light (MIL) to come on and the airbag system to deactivate under certain driving conditions.

The data may become corrupted during a system reboot or power interruption while the system is writing to the ACU’s memory. When this happens, the airbag MIL will illuminate to warn drivers.

As of October 22, 2026, Hyundai is aware of 331 alleged incidents related to the issue. However, none have resulted in a crash, fire, or injury.

Owners can take their vehicle to the closest dealer, where it will update the ACU software, free of charge.

Hyundai expects to mail out owner notification letters on January 1, 2027. In the meantime, owners can contact Hyundai’s customer service at 1-855-371-9460 with any questions or concerns. Hyundai’s recall number is 311.

You can also contact the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration Vehicle Safety Hotline at 1-888-327-4236 or visit the NHTSA.gov website for more information. The NHTSA campaign number is 26V728.

BioWare Documentary Slated for Release in 2027

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Daniel is a lifelong gamer with a love for various genres and gameplay styles who enjoys exploring games and playing from various angles.

It’s been a few weeks now since the sudden announcement of EA’s closure of BioWare, longtime developer of the fan-favorite Mass Effect series. The major fervor surrounding this landmark closure, as well as the cancellation of the next Mass Effect, has gradually started to die down, and all that’s left is a sad little hole in our hearts where hope for a new spacefaring adventure once lived.

In spite of its unfortunate denouement, it doesn’t change the fact that BioWare was, is, and shall always remain a beloved studio, whose stories and worlds touched many a heart. In fact, BioWare and its staff were something of hometown heroes to its origin in the city of Edmonton, Canada. Because of this, a production effort has been spurred forth by the Canadian entertainment industry to properly document the studio’s 30-plus years of history.

For an in-depth look at how we lost one of the gaming greats, this is how the fall of BioWare was years in the making.

According to a Deadline exclusive published today, a documentary outlining the history of BioWare, from its founding in 1995 to its closure this year, is in production now, with hopes to have it up on screens sometime next year, including a run through all of the major film festivals. This will be the definitive BioWare story, told not just by its staff, but the many fans whose lives were changed by its productions.

The documentary, titled “Massive Effect: The BioWare Story,” is being written by Canadian writer and director Akash Sherman, best known for his 2018 sci-fi film, Clara. Interestingly, the actual filming process for this documentary began about two years ago, which means everything was already well underway the moment the decision came down from EA to shutter the studio. Sherman and his crew have captured the moment the BioWare story came to an end, as well as the rippling effects it had on the gaming community.

The actual filming process for this documentary began about two years ago.

The headliners of the documentary are BioWare’s three original founders, Ray Muzyka, Greg Zeschuk, and Augustine Yip, though it’s also slated to feature appearances by a variety of other game industry alumni, including Lucasfilm Games Vice President Douglas Reilly, Star Wars: Knights of the Old Republic director Casey Hudson, Game Awards creator Geoff Keighley, and even EA’s infamous former CEO, John Riccitiello.

A ranking list of all the games BioWare has ever developed in the history of the studio.

“When the production team asked for our blessing to tell the BioWare story, we were honored and deeply moved,” Muzyka and Zeschuk said in a statement obtained by Deadline. “BioWare was never just about the logo or the building. It was about the stories brought to life by the amazing people on our dev teams, and the fans who defended those worlds, championed them and carried them forward.”

The film’s creation and production is something of a love letter from BioWare’s home turf of Canada. In fact, its creation was commissioned by Canada’s Super Channel, with the Canada Media Fund offering support throughout the production.

“We are thrilled to bring Massive Effect: The BioWare Story to Canadian audiences,” Kim Ball, EVP of Content & Communications for Super Channel told Deadline. “As an Edmonton-based company, we’re especially proud to celebrate BioWare’s extraordinary hometown story and the people, creativity and groundbreaking storytelling behind some of the most beloved games of a generation.”

The film’s producers are inviting BioWare’s fans to contribute photos, videos, and stories of how BioWare’s games have affected their lives, with a select few slated to be featured in the film. “BioWare’s fans have been part of its journey since the very first game,” said one of the film’s producers, Adam Scorgie. “It’s only right that they help us tell the rest of the story.”

Sherman himself has similar reverence for BioWare, saying that their games are what helped ignite his initial creative spark, and that it was his “privilege and responsibility to share the journey of underdogs who came together to create deeply beloved storytelling.”

There's still a chance that Mass Effect can survive.

Certificata la ripartenza dopo la mancata qualificazione al Mondiale, la Nazionale tornerà a radunarsi domenica sera a Coverciano in vista delle ultime due gare del girone di Nations League. Il doppio successo ottenuto nella lunga finestra di settembre/ottobre con la Turchia e il pareggio in rimonta nel match di Saint-Denis con la Francia hanno permesso agli Azzurri di portarsi al secondo posto in classifica nel Gruppo 1 della Lega A a quota sette punti, tre in meno della capolista Francia e uno in più del Belgio: e proprio i Bleus e i Diavoli Rossi saranno gli avversari dell’Italia nelle sfide in programma giovedì 12 novembre allo stadio Giuseppe Meazza di Milano (emessi 57.000 biglietti, in vendita gli ultimi 3.000 tagliandi) e domenica 15 novembre allo stadio Re Baldovino di Bruxelles.

Già sicura almeno del terzo posto e di essere quindi tra le teste di serie al sorteggio per le qualificazioni europee in programma il prossimo 6 dicembre a Belfast, l’Italia va a caccia di altri punti per ipotecare la permanenza nella Lega A (le due migliori terze classificate si salvano senza bisogno di ricorrere agli spareggi) e con l’ambizione di chiudere il girone nelle prime due posizioni conquistando così il pass per i quarti di finale.

Se per le prime quattro gare – racchiuse nell’arco di undici giorni – Roberto Mancini aveva puntato su un gruppo allargato di 34 calciatori, stavolta il Ct ne ha convocati 31. Quattro giovani sono alla prima chiamata in Nazionale maggiore: il centrocampista classe 2009 del Como Riccardo Cassano, il centrocampista classe 2008 del Parma Simone Lontani, l’attaccante classe 2006 del Milan Alphadjo Cisse e l’attaccante classe 2007 del Como Mattia Liberali.

Per quanto riguarda Cassano e Lontani, Mancini ha voluto convocarli per poterli osservare da vicino e per dar loro una preziosa opportunità per mettersi in mostra: non resteranno aggregati al gruppo per tutta la durata del raduno, ma nei prossimi giorni raggiungeranno le Nazionali Under 18 e Under 19, impegnate rispettivamente nel Mondiale al via il 19 novembre in Qatar e nella seconda fase delle qualificazioni al Campionato Europeo in programma dall’11 al 17 novembre nelle Marche.

Portieri: Marco Carnesecchi (Atalanta), Gianluigi Donnarumma (Manchester City), Guglielmo Vicario (Juventus);

Difensori: Honest Ahanor (Crystal Palace), Alessandro Bastoni (Inter), Riccardo Calafiori (Arsenal), Federico Dimarco (Inter), Daniele Ghilardi (Roma), Michael Kayode (Brentford), Gianluca Mancini (Roma), Marco Palestra (Chelsea), Giorgio Scalvini (Atalanta);

Centrocampisti: Nicolò Barella (Inter), Riccardo Cassano (Como), Issa Doumbia (Sporting CP), Nicolò Fagioli (Fiorentina), Davide Frattesi (Lazio), Simone Lontani (Parma), Niccolò Pisilli (Roma), Samuele Ricci (Como), Alessandro Romano (Cagliari), Sandro Tonali (Tottenham);

A new Assassin's Creed Valhalla patch fittingly named "The Most Requested Update" just dropped, adding a range of features and systems that players have been asking for, including the fabled New Game Plus mode.

Ubisoft shared the news on the PlayStation Blog, which notes that the PS5 Pro version of Assassin's Creed Valhalla now sports PSSR optimizations "to enhance 4K image quality and deliver our Quality mode at 60FPS on PS5 Pro." More detailed patch notes are available on Steam.

New Game Plus is, as is often the case among modern Assassin's Creed fans, a headliner.

"We know this has been a request for ages, and we appreciate your patience," writes Ubisoft, some four years after Valhalla's release. "You can now start your Viking quest all over again after completing the main story of Assassin’s Creed Valhalla.

"To access NG+ you'll have to complete the Holy Day Quest, which is the last quest of the Hamtunscire region. (This is before the true ending of the game but since it is a very big game, we’ve decided to allow you to start NG+ a little bit earlier) Each iteration of NG+ will get progressively harder, offering new challenges to players who want to revisit the Viking Age again and again."

Note that you will have to save the game post-update to unlock the NG+ option.

With NG+ in mind, the cap on gear upgrades has been increased from 13 to 27, and upgrading is now tied to the new mithril resource. "You will need a lot of it," Ubisoft says.

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

There's more progression to be had, too. "Extended Progression Mode" adds "a more granular sense of progression," by which Ubisoft means gear upgrades and Runes "increase Power by 1 point" while Masteries "increase Power every 5 skill points."

"This mode is intended to increase the impact of your gear. The challenge is greater, but the reward is great for the mighty," the studio explains.

Runes can also, themselves, be upgraded after today's update, and may reach the new "superior" tier. This, too, requires mithril.

"The second addition to the rune forge is in the engrave page; we’ve added more than 300 available engravable runes, allowing players to engrave all of the perks that have been added since the release of Assassin’s Creed Valhalla, including DLCs and those purchased in the Helix Store," the patch notes add.

For all that gear you're hoarding, Valhalla has also added a Stash inventory. "As we all know, Assassin’s Creed Valhalla is a big game, and it keeps getting bigger each year," Ubisoft reflects. So, the kind folks of Ravensthorpe have installed "a newly installed Stash which, when interacted with, will allow players to store any piece of gear and any rune from their inventory."

The long-awaited update is rounded out by two new achievements for completionists (fully upgrade divine gear, and complete NG+), PSSR 2 support for PS5 Pro, and a long list of balance changes "trying to make dual wielding feel better, improve stat runes as well as balancing skill masteries and their bonuses."

Ubisoft says it's done with Assassin's Creed remakes for now after Black Flag Resynced, and Assassin's Creed Hexe is "making steady progress" toward 2027 update

Gray Zone Warfare Update Addresses Biggest Rogue Ops Concerns

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Lauren has experience in the gaming and esports space, writing news, guides, features, reviews, lists, conducting interviews, and more. Before joining the team at DualShockers, Lauren was a Features Writer at Adventure Gamers, a Guides Writer at Gameranx, as well as contributing as a News Writer at PCInvasion and Daily

Gray Zone Warfare stands out for its immersion as a PvE open-world tactical shooter set on a quarantined Southeast Asian island with plenty of narrative elements to get stuck into. Rogue Ops allows for a fresh experience by building your very own Hideout and growing your reputation in new ways.

The ambitious mode has a lot to love, but it has faced its fair share of player complaints, as major issues have been interrupting the fun in the gameplay loop. A Halloween Dev Stream teased significant improvements to enemy AI and making the Hideout economy more rewarding.

This is the detailed all purpose hub for Gray Zone Warfare guides and content.

Now that Gray Zone Warfare Patch 0.5.1.0 is live, we can dive into all the key changes you can expect to see when you embark on your next mission.

First of all, huge changes have been made to the way enemy AI behaves. One of the biggest criticisms players had of the AI enemies was their inconsistency and the feeling of them being extremely overpowered.

Following this update, enemies should no longer know your exact position after shooting. Sound now only gives them an approximate location, meaning the farther away you are, the more difficult it is for them to detect you. Rather than the AI instantly sharing your exact position, they'll rely on information they can gather, which ultimately makes their reactions more natural.

Stealth has also undergone some work to make it more consistent. Moving slowly should make you harder to detect, and the AI exhibits reactions to light that you’d expect. These changes should make stealth, flanking enemies, and repositioning more rewarding and allow you to take a greater advantage over your surroundings.

Improvements have also been made to how enemies engage in combat. Trained soldiers bring more precision to their combat abilities and make better use of cover, but untrained enemies will be more careless in their actions.

Combined with fixing pesky problems with hit boxes, grenades, and enemies getting stuck behind doors, this series of changes aims to make combat more varied and test your tactical performance.

Sound now only gives them an approximate location, meaning the farther away you are, the more difficult it is for them to detect you.

Another major change has been made to the economy in Hideout. A range of rewards have been increased, including raising the rewards from smuggling tasks and increasing the sales prices of smuggling backpacks, and improving rewards from hideout handovers and increasing the sale prices of hideout products.

Production and disassembly times have even been reduced across the board, and the size and weight of selected building materials and disassembly have been reduced for a better looting experience.

The Gray Zone Warfare update arrives with a slew of bug fixes to gameplay, animations, audio, tasks, and so much more. This includes fixing a bug where the health panel could show 100% damage on an organ that was not destroyed and repairing an issue that caused some tasks to not be offered due to a nearby task blocking them. The full patch notes can be viewed here.

In the meantime, the team will continue to address further feedback, like AI enemies seemingly taking too many shots to kill, provided as an example, with further fixes planned for upcoming patches. The developers are already working on their next major update, Patch 0.5.2.0.

Apple and Home Depot join list of objectors to tokenized stock plan pushed by NYSE owner

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An ambitious joint venture  to bring U.S. stocks onto the blockchain appears to be on life support. The venture, cochaired by former New York Gov. Andrew Cuomo, and led by crypto firm OKX and the parent company of the New York Stock Exchange, announced plans last month to offer round-the-clock trading of tokenized shares of 62 public companies. But as of Thursday, 54 of those companies had objected to having their shares traded on the platform.

Aerospace manufacturer Boeing and business software maker ServiceNow joined the objection list on Wednesday, a day after Apple, Home Depot and GE Vernova filed objections. Earlier objectors include some of corporate America’s biggest names, from Microsoft, Alphabet, and Nvidia to JPMorgan Chase and Walmart.

Home Depot declined to comment. Boeing, ServiceNow, Apple, and GE Vernova did not immediately respond to Fortune’s request for comment.

Companies outside OKXICE’s original proposed lineup have also filed objections, including Wells Fargo, Nebius Group, Arm Holdings, Cerebras Systems, and Cigna Group.

The expanded objection list comes a month after Cuomo announced the tokenized stock trading venue in a social media post. The venture was born out of a temporary exemption granted by the Securities and Exchange Commission in September that allows qualifying platforms to offer trading in tokenized stocks without registering as national securities exchanges, provided they meet certain conditions.

The eight companies in OKXICE’s original proposed lineup without a disclosed objection so far predominantly have ties to crypto or blockchain. They include USDC issuer Circle Internet Group, crypto exchange Coinbase, trading platform Robinhood, digital asset custodian BitGo, bitcoin treasury company Strategy and tokenization company Securitize. Software company Palantir and server maker Super Micro Computer round out the list.

OKXICE declined to say whether the growing number of objections would affect its planned launch.

The SEC approved the exemption two days after the Clarity Act, a bill to establish a regulatory framework for digital assets, stalled in the Senate. Under the exemption, platforms like OKXICE do not need a company’s permission to offer trading in tokenized versions of its shares. Instead, they must notify the company and give it 30 days from receipt to object and block the listing, as Fortune reported earlier.

Applied Optoelectronics, a networking equipment maker among the companies to file objections, previously told Fortune that the new framework’s potential impact on its stock’s pricing, liquidity and volatility remained unclear. The company also expressed concern that investors might mistakenly believe it sponsored or endorsed the offering.

“At this stage, we don’t see a clear benefit to AOI or our shareholders that justifies these uncertainties,” a spokesperson said.

Fed, OCC check US banks after Binance hack as experts flag security risks

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

US regulators reportedly contacted banks following last week’s $6.3 billion Binance security hack, while experts raised concerns about transaction security and crypto risks.

US regulators reportedly contacted banks following last week’s $6.3 billion Binance hack, as cybersecurity experts raised concerns about how financial institutions handle crypto-related security risks.

Officials from the Federal Reserve and Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) began contacting banks on Monday to ask about “unusual activity” or service disruptions, Reuters reported Thursday, citing people familiar with the matter.

The reported inquiries could be followed by closer attention to how banks manage crypto-related risks, including custody, counterparty exposure and incident response, according to Stefan Muehlbauer, head of US government affairs at CertiK.

“After an incident of this scale, it would be surprising if the Fed and OCC didn’t check in with the banks they supervise,” Muehlbauer told Cointelegraph. “Their mandate is to protect the financial system, and that means understanding where exposures sit and how quickly they can respond.”

According to Reuters, banks have been assessing the hack and checking their systems to figure out whether they could absorb or manage similar threats.

Reuters’ report also noted that a cybersecurity team at one of the largest US banks contacted Binance within hours of the hack. A Binance spokesperson confirmed with Cointelegraph that the exchange shared its publicly released incident report with all its banking partners and was not aware of any vulnerabilities affecting them.

Muehlbauer explained the procedure currently unfolding is “how a maturing framework should work.” He added that “it’s not a signal that regulators are pulling back from the industry.”

The inquiries also come after the Federal Reserve removed a requirement in April 2025 for state member banks to notify regulators before engaging in crypto activities. The Fed said it would continue overseeing those activities through its regular supervisory process.

A Federal Reserve spokesperson told Cointelegraph that the agency had nothing to share regarding the reported inquiries. Cointelegraph also contacted the OCC for comment but has not heard back at the time of publishing.

Related: Binance traces $6.3B hack to fake job interviews, compromised AI tool

Binance said in its Nov. 3 incident report that approximately $6.34 billion in digital assets were affected by the attack on Oct. 29, with $2.85 billion frozen or intercepted as of the report.

According to the crypto exchange, the attack began with compromised software installed by an engineer in June. Hackers later employed social engineering, stolen credentials and malicious software to get access to wallet systems and carry out unauthorized transfers.

Dr. Darren Williams, founder and CEO of cybersecurity firm BlackFog, told Cointelegraph that the methods used in the attack pose potential risks to traditional banks, particularly those offering crypto custody and payment services.

“Attackers no longer need to break encryption or steal private keys if they can compromise the trusted systems and people authorized to move money,” Williams said.

He warned that hackers could use compromised employee devices, accounts or third-party services to move money without triggering any alerts. He also said the growing use of agentic AI in financial operations could pose even more risks.

“The priority must be stopping unauthorized transactions and data exfiltration before assets or sensitive information leave the organization, not simply investigating the damage afterwards,” Williams said.

Binance has since said that it has strengthened its security controls and was bringing in independent cybersecurity experts to review the attack.

Magazine: Binance hackers raced to move billions before it could be frozen

D&D’s World of Warcraft Designer Hid a Personal Easter Egg in Plain Sight

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The team also obsessively counted toes, banners, and mountain peaks

Mounts in World of Warcraft are status symbols, a way of showing off the challenging raids you’ve completed or how far in the PvP ranks you’ve climbed. Dungeons & Dragons: World of Warcraft lead designer James Wyatt couldn’t help showing off himself a bit in the book’s art, where he slipped in an image of his Night Elf warrior riding the Spirit of Eche'ro. Acquiring the spectral moose required spending hours digging up bone fragments with the Archaeology profession, an activity so tedious it was discontinued after the 2018 expansion Battle for Azeroth.

Fortunately, getting those rare cosmetic items is much easier in the Dungeons & Dragons expansion than in WoW. A character proficient in history can spend an hour or a short rest searching the site of Highmountain Tauren ruins for Archaeology fragments. If they gather six, they can be assembled into a staff that casts the Find Steed spell, summoning the famed moose once ridden by the Tauren hero Huln Highmountain. Wyatt also lets players acquire the Cenarion War Hippogryph, another mount he spent a lot of effort on, by raising their Renown with the Cenarion Circle through protecting the natural balance of Azeroth.

“Cosmetic rewards in WoW are about bragging rights, which is an individual achievement, and D&D is not a game about individual achievements. It's a game about stuff you do as a group together,” Wyatt told Polygon in a virtual interview. “Having mounts appear as Renown rewards and other special things was important for the feel of this cool element of WoW that I want to see in D&D, but the sort of rare achievement aspect of it falls into the category of mechanics that we didn't try to translate.”

Some of the book’s other Easter eggs are nods to WoW’s signature characters, with the Banshee Queen Sylvanas Windrunner appearing on a scale used by a Forsaken alchemist. Others are meant to establish the close ties between WoW and D&D. A gnome is used as the signature Warlock in both D&D: WoW and the Player’s Handbook, and the WoW book’s gnome art depicts them constructing a mechanostrider mount just like the Player’s Handbook version has them building a golem.

Wyatt and lead art director Sergio Jaramillo spent a lot of time playing WoW so they could provide artists with guidelines to ensure the book’s illustrations were true to the game.

“People have seen these places for thousands of hours on their computers,” Jaramillo said. “They know each peak that Icecrown Citadel is supposed to have and how many banners Ogrimmar has. We have to go through it, compare it, triple-check, and then maybe it’s ready. We sent it to Blizzard, and maybe Blizzard catches something we didn’t. A couple of times there were like, ‘We didn’t know this was there. Nice catch.’”

The team wanted to be precise with its modeling of characters, and even carefully counted the fingers and toes for Azeroth’s various species. They wanted to be sure to replicate unusual features, like trolls’ hunched posture, and the full range of eye and hair colors. They would build characters in the Wowhead Dressing Room, a digital tool that lets you recreate any character build and deck it out in equipment, and then commission artists based on the renders. There were times Blizzard couldn’t help them because the models simply didn’t exist in the game. Visit the Tauren city of Thunder Bluff in WoW and you’ll see plenty of Tauren adults and some babies, but no children. Jaramillo’s team had to come up with the proportions for the Tauren kids they wanted to show riding a kodo, the traditional Tauren mount, in the book’s character origins section.

WoW players can be very passionate about their preferences for playing Horde or Alliance, and Wyatt and Jaramillo wanted to make sure they didn’t appear to favor one faction over another. They made sure to have balanced representation across illustrations showcasing everything from the game’s professions to its boss fights. They even put the Collector’s Edition book in a slipcase so players could choose whether to have the Horde or Alliance logo displayed.

Getting the details of another game right was a challenge, but Wyatt said that in many ways, the project was easier than the work he did designing D&D books based on the Magic: The Gathering worlds of Ravnica, Theros, and Strixhaven.

“Worldbuilding on Magic is like building a movie set,” Wyatt said. “It feels like you’re setting up fronts that are held up by two-by-fours in the back, and if you start pushing too deep, you’re going to realize it’s all going to fall over. D&D worldbuilding has to account for characters who can do anything and go anywhere, and so does WoW. It is a very fleshed out world already. The lore is there.”

“The goal here is to recreate D&D in Azeroth, not to recreate the MMO experience.”

Sydie Peck has been in the headlines as of late for his surprising first-half hat-trick against Preston North End in midweek, but the midfielder's displays throughout this campaign deserve more recognition.

The England youth international's ceiling is extraordinarily high, and Sheffield United will be hoping to be the continued beneficiaries of his services for years to come, as time will tell exactly how this saga plays out.

Under the Surface

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Canali gives a behind-the-scenes look at sartorial excellence: the time, precision and mastery that transform raw materials into a jacket made to last

Under the surface, unseen layers lie. They stay there, silent. They don’t demand the spotlight. They don’t seek glory. And yet, like a beating heart, they form the innermost foundation of any real achievement. Where passion, enthusiasm, and discipline meet, the logic of excellence takes shape. Such is the story of Under the Surface, the newest creative collaboration from Canali.

Under the Surface is a look at the unseen architecture of a sartorial jacket by Canali. Every Canali jacket is born from a complex system of about 200 steps, each executed with meticulous care. Around 15 specialists are involved in the process—one that sees more than 520 feet of thread sewn into 22,000 stitches, both machine- and hand-made. Unseen when looking at the finished garment, the process informs each piece’s quality, comfort, shape, and durability.

It’s not just a matter of talent: rather, it’s about staying faithful to one’s goals, pursuing them with dedication even when no one is watching. It’s then that quality truly makes a difference, and the end result becomes the visible manifestation of a long creation process that demands great effort, experience and willpower.

That commitment of excellence unites the sartorial world of Canali and Ethan Hawke, the brand’s new Global Ambassador. The actor, writer, and director has dedicated almost four decades to perfecting his art, with endless, ever-learning curiosity. For their new Fall/Winter 2026 campaign, Canali has decided to capture what goes on behind the scenes. In the film, Hawke pores over his scripts, rehearses lines, refines each motion: a painstaking introspective process made of research, study, and exercise, preceding each of his performances.

The behind-the-scenes of a Canali jacket starts with selecting the finest fabrics, sourced from the best Italian wool mills. Then, each textile is examined and interpreted. While technology now allows extreme precision and control, some assessments are still left to the eye and expertise of human specialists. Even tiny details such as slightly different shades of color or variations in texture can influence the final product.

The fabric is then stabilized via steaming cycles and specific treatments, to ensure its behavior remains consistent over time. Then it’s time for digital mapping, guiding every cutting room decision: here, each of the jacket’s pieces is carefully positioned to also help keep waste to a minimum. It’s the first irreversible step.

The garment’s three-dimensional structure is then shaped upon the canvas. Canali crafts theirs in-house, adapting it to each model’s fit and fabrics. Made from horsehair, plant fibers, and natural reinforcements, the canvas is modeled on custom-made concave shapes, known as Schiena d’Asino (Donkey’s Back), which replicate the chest’s anatomy. The canvas is crucial for determining how a jacket drapes over the body, avoiding stiffness. It’s here that the volume of the chest, the balance of the silhouette and ease of wearing begin to be defined.

Then it’s time to craft the front, lapel, padded shoulder, and the sleeves. From planning to execution, nothing’s left to chance. The tailor observes, corrects, iterates. It’s a patient process, requiring time, knowledge, and skill. Lastly, the various layers are united through basting, stitching, and ironing processes. As these details interact, the garment takes on its structure and sophisticated character.

Something special also takes place behind the scenes with Hawke: day after day, the actor’s preparation subtly transforms his thoughts and behavior until, almost by instinct, he takes on each role’s intimate characteristics.

Time underpins the whole process. After being assembled and pressed, the jacket is allowed to rest, to dissipate the steam and stabilize the cloth. This is how the fabric finds its balance, and the garment gains a sort of memory: the ability to naturally find its shape again, even after having been worn, folded, or packed into a suitcase.

In over 90 years of heritage, Canali has refined their techniques through repetition, experience, and experimentation. Each garment is built upon the time-tested values of care, know-how, precision. The Canali style is not one made of grand gestures, but daily practice, intimate and subtle: the constant research into a shape that lasts. One hero of the Under the Surface project is the vicuña jacket, available exclusively at selected flagship boutiques by Canali.The campaign, and the collaboration between Canali and Ethan Hawke, speak to the art of shaping oneself, guided by an ambition to do things well with patience and self-awareness. Both in tailoring and in cinema, there is so much quiet work that happens behind the scenes to make creativity possible. Discipline and rigour do not constrain creative freedom; they make it possible. For Canali, excellence takes shape through the craftsmanship with which each element is made. For Ethan Hawke, it is the preparation that allows him to approach each role with confidence.And, as the Under The Surface campaign reveals, each of these steps adds something essential to the finished product. In the case of the Canali cashmere and vicuña blazer (the hero style of the FW26 campaign), it is structure, movement, character, durability, and a lightness that can only be achieved through the combination of expertly-chosen materials and complex fine tailoring. The knowledge required to execute this is almost tangible, felt in the quality of the piece.

The finished result only has value because of the strong, unseen structure underneath. It is a story we can all resonate with. Little by little, layer by layer, day by day, we each build ourselves in pursuit of excellence. Our actions create the people we are – even, or especially, when nobody is watching – in the same way that the apparent simplicity of a piece of clothing hides the depth of work required to craft it. This is the essence of Under the Surface.

Strategy’s $100 STRC comeback could turn a cash drain into fresh Bitcoin buying power

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After about $2 billion in buybacks, STRC reached the price at which Strategy could sell new shares to help finance Bitcoin purchases.

Strategy's STRC preferred stock briefly returned to $100 on Nov. 4, potentially restoring a funding channel for Bitcoin purchases after months of cash-consuming share buybacks.

The company announced on Nov. 4 that its STRC security had reached $100, prompting executive chairman Michael Saylor to declare:

The recovery followed about $2 billion in share repurchases since July, as Strategy sought to support the preferred stock after its prolonged decline below its $100 stated value.

Independent tracker STRC.live reported that the security touched $100 before retreating to close at $99.97. It also estimated that renewed at-the-market (ATM) activity may have financed the purchase of approximately 191 BTC on Wednesday.

Strategy has not confirmed those purchases.

Strategy's Bitcoin treasury stood at 849,327 BTC as of Nov. 1.

The $100 threshold matters because Strategy’s current policy is not to issue STRC below that level. Its Nov. 2 disclosure listed about $18 billion of STRC available for issuance and sale as of Nov. 1. New sales could help finance Bitcoin purchases without issuing additional common stock.

However, months of below-par trading constrained that approach, forcing Strategy to shift from raising capital through STRC to repurchasing the security at a discount.

STRC's rebound followed a significant change to its dividend structure, with Strategy introducing daily distributions from Nov. 1 to improve the security's price stability and appeal to income-seeking investors.

The preferred stock, which currently carries a 12% annual dividend rate, returned to $100 within three trading sessions of the change.

Strategy CEO Phong Le had previously described STRC as “Digital Credit for everyone,” highlighting its growth into a $10 billion product within its first year and average daily trading volume of approximately $143 million.

He also said yield-generating stablecoins and tokenized products built on STRC had grown to $500 million, illustrating the company's ambitions to expand the instrument beyond its immediate Bitcoin financing operations.

The broader significance of such securities was highlighted in an Oct. 20 report by Fidelity Digital Assets, which identified perpetual preferred stock as an emerging source of institutional Bitcoin demand.

Fidelity estimated that Bitcoin-focused preferred securities issued by Strategy and other companies had financed the accumulation of 135,979 BTC as of Oct. 5.

The research argued that these instruments allow companies to attract investors seeking regular income and channel the proceeds into Bitcoin, creating a funding mechanism distinct from spot exchange-traded funds.

However, Fidelity warned that the model ultimately depends on Bitcoin appreciating sufficiently to offset the cost of paying dividends, exposing issuers to financial pressure during prolonged market downturns.

Strategy's recent experience illustrates that vulnerability. Although the company has maintained STRC's 12% dividend rate, it spent billions supporting the stock when market demand proved insufficient to keep it near its intended trading level.

Investors can check Strategy's next weekly filing for STRC share sales, Bitcoin purchases and the pace of buybacks.

Last month we got news that Wing Commander 3, the FMV space-combat sim with perhaps the highest concentration of Mark Hamill in any videogame, would be getting a remaster. Producer Jim Vessella is heading up the project, and revealed it during a presentation at the Portland Retro Gaming Expo.

You know what? I'm inclined to believe it. EA says the remasters are the fruit of "the discovery of a physical media vault containing thousands of Betacam, VHS, and D1 tapes alongside other media assets", while "the digitization process recovered the supposedly lost Master FMV sequences for these seven legendary titles, which were meticulously re-edited to restore the over 1,500 cinematic scenes across these games".

Walmart Will Have Three Major Shopping Events This Holiday Season

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you're thinking of picking up any big-ticket items soon—whether for yourself or as a gift for someone on your list— I would advise you to wait until you see what retailers have in store for the holiday sales season, which will kick off in earnest in mid-November. Walmart is the first major retailer to announce its Black Friday and Cyber Monday plan for 2026, and they actually involve three major shopping events between now and the December holidays.

As it did in 2025, Walmart will hold three sales between Nov. 12 and Dec. 2, as outlined in a recent press release. The sales will be both online and in-store, and will target a mix of Walmart+ members and general shoppers.

The events will take place on three different dates:

Walmart's Black Friday Deals Event 1: Nov. 12–15

Available online and in stores starting at 6 a.m. local time

Walmart+ members get six hours of early access beginning online at 12:01 a.m. ET on November 12

Walmart's Black Friday Deals Event 2: Nov. 23–29

Online only: Nov. 23 starting at 12:01 a.m. ET

Online and in stores: Nov. 27 starting at 6 a.m. local time

Walmart+ members get six hours of early access online beginning Nov. 23 at 12:01 a.m. ET

Walmart's Cyber Monday Deals Event: Nov. 30-Dec. 2

Walmart says that you can expect discounts on electronics, toys, fashion, beauty, home goods, gifts, and everyday essentials. Although the company doesn't provide much in the way of specifics in its press release, last year's sales included discounts on TCL TVs, Dyson vacuums, Dreo appliances, and toys from LEGO.

Walmart already has plenty of good deals out early. You don't need to wait for the official sale to start to find some products going for their lowest prices ever. Here are some of the best ones I've found so far:

The holiday season will see major sales from the same list of retailers that compete for your dollars throughout the year—namely, Best Buy, Target, and Amazon. Each will have its own version of Black Friday and Cyber Monday sales. So far, only Walmart has revealed its plans.

In short, yes. Though they aren't quite as impactful as they once were, Black Friday and Cyber Monday sales offer discounts that can be rare throughout the rest of the year. Whether it's a major purchase you've been saving up for or you’re shopping for gifts, it’s a good time to look for discounts, especially on items like appliances and electronics. Last year's sales included categories such as tech, home improvement, and fitness.

Black Friday used to be when major retailers offered their best deals on expensive tech and appliances, while Cyber Monday was for cheaper tech and gave smaller businesses a chance to compete online. Now, however, the distinction is almost meaningless: Every major retailer offers sales on both days, which means the smart move is to know what you want, use price trackers to monitor the deals, and refer to our Black Friday coverage, where we do all the searching, curating, and price tracking for you.

On this day in space! Nov. 5, 2002: NASA's Galileo spacecraft flies by Jupiter moon Almathea only to find it's a 'pile of icy rubble less dense than water'

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.

Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The Galileo spacecraft was running on cosmic fumes by the time it reached Amalthea and the flyby would be a last of its kind.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

On Nov. 5, 2002, NASA's old Galileo spacecraft was almost out of fuel. But the Jupiter mission still had some science to do. Galileo flew by a small moon called Amalthea. Amalthea is small, but it's interesting for scientists.

The Jupiter moon Amalthea is the reddest object in our solar system. It also generates more heat than it gets from the sun. Scientists think Amalthea creates electrical currents as it orbits in Jupiter's huge magnetic field. Near this little moon, strong radiation from Jupiter is dangerous for spacecraft.

The flyby caused some problems for Galileo. The spacecraft went into safe mode only 30 minutes after going past Amalthea. NASA said it was ready for problems, because Galileo was on its last legs, and the radiation near Amalthea was strong. However, NASA quickly fixed the problem and got Galileo working again.

Less than a year later, Galileo threw itself into Jupiter's atmosphere. The spacecraft was crushed by the high pressure in Jupiter's atmosphere, but its data still survives on Earth.

The Galileo spacecraft was running on cosmic fumes by the time it reached Amalthea and the flyby would be the last time the probe would ever swing by one of Jupiter's moons.

While it may not look like it in Galileo's black and white images above and below, Amalthea is tinted red due sulfur contamination on its surface. The moon is small, about 168 miles long, and orbits about 112,468 miles (181,000 km) from Jupiter (much closer to the planet than the Earth and its moon).

Galileo flew within 99 miles of Amalthea during its flyby, which also marked the closest Galileo would get to Jupiter before its death dive in September 2005.

"Scientists studying data from NASA's Galileo spacecraft have found that Jupiter's moon Amalthea is a pile of icy rubble less dense than water," NASA announced in May 2005

"Amalthea is throwing us a curve ball," Torrence Johnson, a study co-author on the Almathea flyby and project scientist for the Galileo mission at JPL, said in 2005. "Its density is well below that of water ice, and even with substantial porosity, Amalthea probably contains a lot of water ice, as well as rock."

Originally known as Jupiter V (because it was the fifth Jupiter moon discovered after the so-called Galilean moons Io, Europa, Ganymede, Callisto), Amalthea was named after naiad, or water nymph, from Greek mythology.

It is fitting that Amalthea was the last of Jupiter's moons to be visited by the Galileo spacecraft. The moon was also last Jupiter moon to be discovered by visual observations when it was first disovered by astronomer Edward Emerson Barnard in September 1892 using the Lick Observatory telescope in California.

The Galileo spacecraft ultimately crashed into Jupiter on Sept. 21, 2005, less than a year after its Amalthea flyby.

Want more space history? Check out our full On This Day In Space Story archive and watch our On This Day In Space videos on YouTube.

On this day in space! Nov. 4, 2015: A rail-launched Super Strypi rocket carrying 13 baby satellites failed during its debut launch for the US Air Force

As an editor with over 10 years of experience in science journalism she has previously written for Scholastic Classroom Magazines, MedPage Today and The Joint Institute for Computational Sciences at Oak Ridge National Laboratory. After studying physics at the University of Tennessee in her hometown of Knoxville, she earned her graduate degree in Science, Health and Environmental Reporting (SHERP) from New York University. Hanneke joined the Space.com team in 2016 as a staff writer and producer, covering topics including spaceflight and astronomy. She currently lives in Seattle, home of the Space Needle, with her cat and two snakes. In her spare time, Hanneke enjoys exploring the Rocky Mountains, basking in nature and looking for dark skies to gaze at the cosmos.

GTA 6 release date: Is there early access? The soonest anyone can play.

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After more than a decade of waiting, gamers will finally get to play Grand Theft Auto VI in just a few weeks. GTA 6 officially comes out on Nov. 19, 2026.

However, that's still too far away for some. GTA fans want to play the game now, or at least as soon as possible. So, when is that? Is there early access for GTA 6?

If you're hoping to be among the first, unfortunately, it's too late for that. Some lucky video game reviewers are reportedly already playing the game in anticipation of their coverage being published alongside its release. The embargo for GTA 6 reviews lifts one day before the release date, on Nov. 18. Some reviewers received their codes late last month, and more are going out to gaming and tech news outlets now and over the next couple of weeks.

What about the average consumer? How can they play the game at the earliest possible date?

Unfortunately, there is no early access for GTA 6. However, if you pre-order the digital version of the game, you'll be able to download GTA 6 on Nov. 10, a full 9 days before its release.

Now, the pre-order won't let you play the game on Nov. 10. The game will remain locked until Nov. 19. But GTA 6 is a massive download. It's reportedly 212 GB. That could take hours to download and install based on your internet connection. By pre-ordering, the game will be ready to play on your gaming console at midnight local time on Nov. 19, giving you the ability to jump into the game the moment it's officially released.

Still not good enough for you? What if you could trick your console into thinking you're in a country where the GTA 6 release comes roughly 18 hours earlier? Well, you can. Change your console region settings to New Zealand and connect to the internet through a VPN, and you'll be able to play GTA 6 on Nov. 18.

Or you can always hop on a flight and take a trip to New Zealand yourself.

Wall Street's fear gauge surges the most since April. Why traders think it should be even higher

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Whether it's a referendum on the midterms or just a continuation of a bear market in semiconductors that dates back to the group's peak in June, one thing's clear from the options action Thursday: few traders saw this coming.

That's evident in the surge in Cboe Volatility index (VIX), which jumped as much as 5.7 points to above 28 about 90 minutes after the opening bell on Thursday. It's the highest reading on VIX since March 31, the day after the S&P 500 bottomed after selling off as tensions between Iran, Israel and the U.S. reached fever pitch.

From its intraday low to high Thursday, VIX moved more than 11 points, the biggest one-day move since April 9. Yet with the S&P 500 down 3%, there's an argument to be made that volatility should be higher. Traders use a rule of thumb that 16 points for the VIX equate to a 1% move in the index.

The S&P 500 was headed for its worst decline since April 2025.

"My data says to de-risk, but my gut says this is a blowout that could create a dip-buying opportunity" said Noel Smith, "I do not see macro stuff causing this, like oil or rates. Those things are relatively unchanged. The tape looks like a liquidation and that's exactly what it is according to what I'm hearing with regards to a hedge fund."

Looking at Wednesday's response to the selloff in semiconductors adds a wrinkle for traders that pointed to a too-calm VIX despite Wednesday's tech-stock wreck. With the VanEck Semiconductor ETF down 3%, it was the first day in market history that the VIX concurrently fell more than 4 points, according to data from the X account "OddStats." As of midday, nearly 800,000 calls traded on the VIX, 3.5 times higher than 30-day average, according to Cboe data.
